HPS Gaussian-Process Resonance Search Analysis Note1
Version 4.9.7: full-2016 support qualiﬁcation and recombination gate2
Emrys Peets
Stanford University and SLAC National Accelerator Laboratory

September 2, 20266
Abstract7
This note describes a Gaussian-process search for narrow prompt e+e− resonances in HPS8
data. Version 4.9.7 adds a source-conditioned test of the lower GP-support edge for full-20169
inference and a post-unblinding audit of the earlier 2021 65 MeV feature. The accepted v4.210
combination and the v4.9.5 2021-only scan remain distinct historical result states.11
The 2016 study uses the pre-existing 10% development sample/subset for partial observed-12
shape information. Before the support decision, the full-2016 array enters only through one13
scalar normalization over 26–210 MeV; no support-speciﬁc full-data ﬁtted amplitude, local p0,14
or upper limit enters selection. The hybrid generating mean also contains an explicitly waived15
broad-tail component whose archived ROOT ﬁt record has fit_ok=false. It is used only as a16
conditional stress truth, not as a qualiﬁed physical background truth or coverage generator.17
Phase 1 tests lower edges from 28 through 34 MeV at 44, 49, 54, and 59 MeV using 25 paired18
backgrounds and matched 0-, 2-, and 5- σA injections. Every edge has only 3 of 12 cell means19
below 0.75 in magnitude and only 1 of 4 background-only means below 0.75; every edge violates20
the predeclared 1.25 gross-bias guard. A uniform analytic-mean diagnostic shows the same21
alternating mass-dependent mismatch without Poisson ﬂuctuations. The frozen rule therefore22
returns no provisional edge and requires the study to stop without retuning.23
Consequently no 75-background continuation, support-speciﬁc full-2016 observed scan, up-24
dated combined upper limit on ε2, or 100-toy combined band is produced. Their absence is an25
enforced analysis decision, not a zero result. Separately, the unchanged 2021 counts at 65 MeV26
give local asymptotic Z = 4. 252 with the v4.2 support and 2.403 with the v4.9.5 support. The27
support-dependent GP state assigns more continuum to the masked region and leaves a smaller28
narrow-signal coeﬃcient. The defensible conclusion is that the narrow-signal attribution is not29
robust to the GP support prescription, not that the feature has been proved background or30
signal has been excluded.31

Change Log183
This page records the analysis-level changes between circulated versions of the note. It is not a184
substitute for the conﬁguration and ﬁgure provenance given in the body and appendices. Numerical185
results from diﬀerent versions should not be combined: each version corresponds to a distinct,186
internally consistent analysis state.187
Table 1: Analysis-note version history. The v4.2 row deﬁnes the accepted analysis result and the
subsequent rows deﬁne the additional diagnostic scope of the present document. Earlier versions
are summarized to keep changes in inputs, validation, and statistical interpretation traceable to
reviewers.
Version Circulation date Principal changes
v1 March–May 2026 Established the full-range Gaussian-process background model, the
blinded local signal-extraction likelihood, the radiative-fraction conversion
to ε2, and the ﬁrst single-dataset and combined validation studies. The
ﬁgures in this version were development results and did not deﬁne the
present candidate conﬁguration.
v2 June 2026 Introduced the 2. 25 σm blind/training geometry, matched-reference
full-reﬁt pseudoexperiments, the shared- ε2 simultaneous likelihood, and
expanded 2015, 2016, and 2021 method documentation. This version used
common or development-stage length-scale choices and retained broader
engineering-run scan ranges in several studies.
v3 July 10, 2026 Uses the regenerated 2021 1% high- psum histogram, with no vertex-ﬁt χ2
requirement and at least eight electron-track hits; restricts the 2015 and
2016 scans to 19 MeV to 90 MeV and 39 MeV to 180 MeV; freezes the
dataset-dependent lower length-scale factors at 1.0, 0.9, and 1.1; and
adopts 90% CL upper limits obtained with CL s. The main validation
section now contains the functional-form seed construction and the
injected-signal lower-bound scans, while superseded closure and limit
studies are retained with their original context in the appendices. The
results section documents the individual and simultaneous limits, local
and approximate global p-values, and all remaining pre-unblinding audit
qualiﬁcations.
v4 August 3, 2026 Separates the GP ﬁt support from the signal-search interval in all three
campaigns. The 2015 and 2016 searches keep their reviewed mass grids
but regain sidebands beyond both endpoints, while the 2021 10% sample
retains the wider support developed in the recent endpoint study. The
result now uses the full 2015 and 2016 datasets in the shared- ε2 ﬁt and
includes conditional background-only expected bands. The same
pseudoexperiments place the observed limit within the ensemble through
the strong-, weak-, and bounded two-sided consistency tests used in v3.
Those quantities remain upper-limit diagnostics rather than discovery
signiﬁcances or a substitute for full-procedure coverage.

Table 1 continued from the previous page.
Version Circulation date Principal changes
v4.1 August 4–5, 2026 Responds to the full-2016 length-scale saturation with a controlled
observed-only scan of kmax,2016 = 8, 10, 12, 15, and 20. Factor 12 is the
ﬁrst nonbinding value and is followed by a stable plateau. The exact
combined observed limit and local asymptotic p0 scan are recomputed
with all other analysis settings ﬁxed. A ten-toy 2021 source-and-exposure
screen—native 1%, 1% × 10, 1% × 100, native 10%, and 10% × 10—is
included only as a ﬁt-space hyperparameter pilot. Its reviewed
background-only scan supports factor 20 for the two projected-100% lanes
and factor 25 if one bound must cover every pilot exposure; the
ﬁxed-amplitude screen shows no factor-20 sensitivity loss, while a common
2–3% under-response remains an unresolved ten-toy closure diagnostic.
No new expected bands, limit-tail ensemble result, toy-calibrated
discovery claim, or coverage claim is included; the observed result remains
a candidate pending production-matched closure and direct coverage. The
August 5 methodology revision adds explanatory schematics for C, ℓ,
ﬁxed αi, and the per-hypothesis optimization; it changes no conﬁguration
or numerical result.
v4.2 August 5, 2026 Accepts kmax,2016 = 12 as the current full-2016 setting and completes the
232-mass combined 90% CL s search with exactly 300 conditional
background-only joint pseudoexperiments at each mass. Central 68% and
95% intervals are reported for that principal shared- ε2 search. The note
also adds standalone observed limits for the individual 2015 full, 2016 full,
and 2021 10% distributions, auxiliary 100-pseudoexperiment standalone
and pairwise conditional limit bands, the combined strong-, weak-, and
bounded two-sided limit-tail diagnostics, and combined and constituent
local asymptotic p0 plots. The auxiliary ensembles select indices 0–99
from the accepted 300-draw parent stream and reuse campaign
pseudo-spectra across scopes. No scan-toy-calibrated global discovery
probability or direct coverage claim is introduced; all bands remain
descriptive quantiles conditional on the reviewed ﬁxed GP states. The
results section also adds an exact ﬁxed-state 65 MeV extraction, including
sideband context, background-subtracted spectra, conditional
Pearson-residual diagnostics, and a comparison of the three standalone
signal estimates with the simultaneous shared- ε2 ﬁt.
v4.2
follow-up
August 6, 2026 Adds the observed-equivalent comparison with the published BaBar
visible 90% limit while scaling only the 2021 count-density response from
10% to 100% statistics. It adds an exact ﬁxed-mass 0.5 MeV
common-binning reﬁt and 1.25 MeV stress test after documenting why
exact 0.625 MeV aggregation is not possible from the histogram-only 2015
and 2016 inputs. The Figure 62 coeﬃcient panel now uses the
physical-domain nominal asymptotic proﬁle set instead of a symmetric
signed-Wald bar. A separate appendix records two ﬁxed-seed,
background-only conditional replacements of the 2021 central window,
their complete observed/asymptotic scans, and optimizer-repeat ledgers.
A deterministic central-mean follow-up separates the functional
truth/GP-analysis shoulder below 65 MeV from the additional ﬂuctuation
in its ﬁxed draw. Ten paired ﬁxed-GP-mean replacements compare the
exactly equivalent 2.25/2.5- σm production-bin scope with a 3- σm scope,
reporting only descriptive observed-limit and local- p0 summaries. This
addendum changes no accepted v4.2 card or principal result, adds no
projected bands or projected p-values, and introduces no coverage or
scan-calibrated global-signiﬁcance claim.

August 7, 2026 Deﬁnes an operational eight-feature catalogue from the three standalone,
three genuine-overlap pairwise, and all-three v4.2 upper-limit curves. For

1 Introduction188
The Heavy Photon Search (HPS) prompt-resonance program is formulated in the minimal visible189
dark-photon model, in which a new broken U (1)D gauge boson mixes kinetically with the Standard-190
Model photon [ 1, 2]. After diagonalizing the gauge ﬁelds, the interaction terms relevant for the191
prompt A′→ e+e− search can be written schematically as192
L ⊃ − 1
4 F ′
µν F ′µν − ε

2 m2

µA′µ + εeJµ

µ. (1)
Here ε is the kinetic-mixing parameter, mA′ is the dark-photon mass, and J µ
EM is the ordinary193
electromagnetic current. In the low-mass region considered here, the minimal visible model predicts194
a narrow prompt dielectron resonance. Its natural width is negligible compared with the HPS195
detector resolution, so the observed line shape is set by the experimental mass resolution [ 3, 4].196
This narrow-width regime makes a resonance search well suited to the 10 MeV to 300 MeV mass197
window: ﬁxed-target electroproduction can reveal a visibly decaying vector as a localized excess198
above the smooth trident spectrum, in a coupling range that includes established dark-sector and199
lepton-anomaly benchmarks.200
In the ﬁxed-target approximation relevant for HPS, prompt dark-photon production is normal-201
ized to the radiative trident rate,202
σ(eZ → eZA′) ≃ 3πε2

the analysis extracts a narrow signal yield from the smooth prompt e+e− spectrum and then203
converts that yield into a limit or best ﬁt for ε2 [2, 3, 4]. The physics interpretation rests on204
the minimal prompt A′model and the radiative-trident normalization; Gaussian-process regression205
(GPR) provides the statistical description of the smooth background.206
1.1 Published HPS prompt-resonance benchmarks207
The published HPS prompt searches provide the immediate experimental context for the current208
GPR analysis. The 2015 engineering-run publication analyzed 1170 nb− 1 at 1. 056 GeV over 19 MeV209
to 81 MeV and reported a lowest local p-value of 1. 7 × 10− 3 at 37. 7 MeV; after the look-elsewhere210
correction the global p-value was 17% [3]. The 2016 prompt search analyzed the full engineering-run211
sample, 10 608 nb− 1 at 2. 3 GeV, over 39 MeV to 179 MeV. Its lowest local p-value was 6. 38 × 10− 3212
at 94 MeV, with a global signiﬁcance of about 1 σ, and it propagated mass-resolution and radiative-213
fraction systematics explicitly into the ﬁnal prompt limit [ 4]. Figure 1 collects those published HPS214
prompt benchmarks in the compact form most relevant for the present GPR note.215

1.2 Complementary prompt-visible constraints216
The relevant comparison is not only which experiments reach the same mass window, but also how217
their exclusions populate the (mA′, ε 2) plane over the broader low-mass visible-dark-photon range.218
In that space HPS occupies a distinct ﬁxed-target niche between very-low-mass beam-dump and219
meson-decay constraints and the higher-mass collider reach. Figure 2 shows that context using220

proximation is not a recalculation of either published likelihood and is not used in the v3 upper225
limit.226

1.3 Motivation for the present GPR analysis227
Gaussian-process regression is best viewed here as a modern form of covariance-based interpolation.228
The historical starting point is kriging, developed in mine-valuation and geostatistical applications229
by Krige and formalized by Matheron: given observations of a smooth ﬁeld at some locations, the230
task is to predict the ﬁeld elsewhere while carrying the uncertainty induced by ﬁnite sampling and231
spatial correlation [ 22, 23]. In a resonance search the coordinate is not geographic position but232
invariant mass. The same statistical idea is useful because the prompt trident continuum is smooth233
on the scale of the HPS mass resolution, while a prompt A′ would appear as a narrow localized234
excess. GPR replaces an explicit closed-form background curve with a probability distribution235
over smooth functions, speciﬁed by a mean and covariance kernel and conditioned on the observed236
training bins outside the mass-dependent excluded window [ 24]. For each test mass, the practical237
output is therefore not only a background prediction in the blinded window, but a correlated238
uncertainty on that prediction.239
14

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
Figure 3: Cartoon of the per-test-mass GPR workﬂow. For each test mass, the GP is trained on
bins across the full mass range except for a narrow excluded window centered on that mass. The
trained GP predicts the posterior mean and covariance inside the excluded window, where a narrow
A′-like signal hypothesis is overlaid only for illustration.
This viewpoint is attractive for HPS for the same reason emphasized in the 2017 high-energy-240
physics study of Frate et al. : ﬁxed empirical functions restrict the background to a low-dimensional241
family, and discrepancies that are hidden by statistical ﬂuctuations can become limiting as lumi-242
nosity grows or as the scan region is widened [ 25]. Figure 4 reproduces three of their comparison243
plots. In toy spectra built from ATLAS dijet data, the GP background model maintains a near-244
unit goodness-of-ﬁt scale and remains stable as the eﬀective luminosity increases, while the ad-hoc245
functional form degrades. The lesson for HPS is not that a GP is assumption-free: the kernel,246
training exclusion, transform, and length-scale bounds are all analysis choices. The beneﬁt is that247
the smoothness assumption is made explicit and propagated as a covariance, rather than being248
hidden in a hand-selected analytic form.249
The later CMS-oriented GPR signal-extraction study by Gandrakota et al. provides the closest250
methodological bridge to the present implementation: choose a kernel class, condition the GP on251
binned count data, compare candidate kernels against the smooth background structure, and then252
separate a localized signal component from the inferred background in a reproducible statistical253
workﬂow [26]. The HPS analysis adopts that framework in a form matched to a ﬁxed-target prompt-254
resonance search. It trains the GP in log-mass/log-count space, removes the test-mass window from255
training, ties the allowed length scale to the detector resolution, converts the posterior prediction256
back into count space, and carries the resulting mean vector and covariance matrix into the local257
proﬁle likelihood. As detailed in Section 4, GPR turns smooth-background interpolation into an258
explicit probabilistic model that can be validated, proﬁled, and combined across datasets.259
Earlier HPS prompt searches used mass-window-speciﬁc background models, window-by-260
window optimization, and a resonance extraction whose numerical behavior depended on the local261
ﬁt boundaries. Here, every mass hypothesis is assigned its own GP ﬁt on a ﬁxed, dataset-speciﬁc262
15

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
(a) Toy-spectrum mean and ±1σ background bands.
(b) Toy goodness-of-ﬁt distributions.
 (c) Goodness of ﬁt versus eﬀective luminosity.
Figure 4: Methodological comparison between Gaussian-process and ad-hoc functional-form back-
ground modeling reproduced from Ref. [ 25]. The panels show that, in the 2017 dijet toy study, the
GP retained an accurate smooth-background description while the ﬁxed functional form became
increasingly strained as the eﬀective statistical precision improved.
16

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
support, with a narrow region around that hypothesis excluded from training. The ﬁt supplies263
a mean vector and covariance matrix for the blinded bins. Signal extraction, local signiﬁcance,264
modiﬁed-frequentist CL s limits, radiative-fraction conversion, and the shared- ε2 combination then265
follow within one statistical framework.266
This change is especially valuable for the present HPS program because one statistical workﬂow267
must describe three campaigns with diﬀerent mass ranges and detector conﬁgurations. In v4 the268
tested signal hypotheses span 19 MeV to 90 MeV for 2015, 39 MeV to 180 MeV for 2016, and 50 MeV269
to 250 MeV for 2021, while the GP ﬁt supports extend beyond those tested intervals to 14 MeV to270
135 MeV, 30 MeV to 210 MeV, and 40 MeV to 300 MeV, respectively. A stable GP model therefore271
matters both for numerical smoothness and for carrying the same radiative-fraction and uncertainty272
treatment across datasets without sacriﬁcing narrow-resolution sensitivity. It also motivates the273
combined analysis: the minimal visible A′ model predicts one common coupling ε2 at a given274
mass, while each HPS run maps that coupling into a diﬀerent expected signal yield through its275
own integrated luminosity, acceptance, resolution, and radiative normalization. The statistical276
combination therefore treats ε2 as the shared parameter of interest and multiplies the independent277
dataset likelihoods, proﬁling the dataset-speciﬁc background nuisance parameters separately. In278
this common-parameter construction, datasets with better local signal-to-background leverage carry279
more information, while weaker channels still contribute correctly; the gain in sensitivity comes280
from adding independent likelihood information for the same minimal-model signal rather than281
from averaging separate limits after the fact.282
1.4 Scope of this note283
This note documents the HPS prompt A′ → e+e− GPR analysis. It emphasizes three related284
physics inputs: the detector apparatus that deﬁnes the mass resolution and acceptance, the dataset-285
speciﬁc radiative-fraction normalization that converts signal yield into ε2, and the proﬁle-likelihood286
methodology used to quote local signiﬁcance and upper limits. The 2015 data are fully unblinded287
and are used as the most mature validation sample. The v4 result combines the full 2015 and288
2016 samples with the reviewed 2021 10% sample in one shared-coupling likelihood. The 2021289
contribution remains a staged partial-data result rather than the ﬁnal full-2021 unblinding.290
The 2019 physics run is mentioned only as part of the detector evolution from the 2015/2016291
baseline conﬁguration to the 2019/2021 upgraded conﬁguration. Its data do not enter the v4292
prompt-resonance analysis.293
Three analysis states are kept distinct. The accepted v4.2 three-campaign analysis uses a 40–294
300 MeV GP support for the 2021 sample. The later v4.9.5 pass is a separate 2021-only analysis295
of the native 10% sample: it retains the 50–250 MeV search interval and freezes a 36–300 MeV296
GP support after the support-edge continuation. Version 4.9.7 then attempts an analogous full-297
2016 support qualiﬁcation but stops in Phase 1 because no eligible edge passes the frozen practical298
rule. It therefore produces no new full-2016 observed or combined result. The accompanying post-299
unblinding audit does revise the interpretation of the unchanged 2021 65 MeV feature: its ﬁtted300
narrow-signal attribution is not robust to the GP-support prescription. None of these conditional301
support studies supplies direct coverage or a scan-global signiﬁcance.302
17

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
2 HPS apparatus and data sets303
2.1 Detector conﬁgurations for the prompt search304
HPS is a forward spectrometer optimized for narrow e+e− resonances and displaced vertices in the305
high-rate environment immediately downstream of a thin tungsten target. Its prompt A′→ e+e−306
sensitivity is set primarily by three subsystems: the Silicon Vertex Tracker (SVT), which deter-307
mines the mass and vertex resolution; the electromagnetic calorimeter (ECal), which provides the308
trigger and track–cluster matching; and the beamline/target geometry, which deﬁnes the forward309
acceptance and the occupancy environment [ 27, 4].310
The 2015 and 2016 engineering runs used the baseline six-station SVT. In that conﬁguration,311
six axial/stereo tracking stations were distributed between z = 10 cm to 90 cm downstream of the312
target, with the ﬁrst active silicon only about 1. 5 mm from the beam plane [ 28, 27]. The ECal313
geometry used in the engineering runs was already close to the ﬁnal HPS layout: two PbWO 4314
crystal halves with the highest-rate crystals nearest the beam removed, leaving the beam to pass315
between the two halves [ 28, 27].316
Before the ﬁrst physics run in 2019, HPS upgraded the front tracker. The 2019 and 2021317
conﬁgurations added a seventh double layer at z = 5 cm and used thin, slim-edge sensors with318
short split striplets [ 27, 29]. Moving the ﬁrst precision measurements closer to the beam reduced319
material and occupancy while improving acceptance for low-mass, small-opening-angle pairs. The320
ECal crystal layout was unchanged, but a positron hodoscope was installed before the 2019 run to321
support a single-arm trigger for events near the ECal beam gap [ 27]. Figure 5 compares the tracker322
layouts used in the engineering and physics runs.323
18

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
(a) Baseline six-layer SVT used in the 2015 and 2016 engineering runs [ 28].
(b) Front-layer upgrade used in the 2019 and 2021 physics runs, including the added
seventh double layer and slim-edge front sensors [ 27, 29].
Figure 5: SVT conﬁgurations used in the prompt-resonance analyses. The 2015/2016 baseline
tracker and the 2019/2021 tracker diﬀer in both layer count and the design of the innermost
stations. The ECal crystal layout was unchanged; the physics-run conﬁguration added a positron
hodoscope.
2.2 Data-taking campaigns and analysis scope324
This note keeps three related states separate. The accepted v4.2 combined result uses the full 2015325
and 2016 engineering-run samples together with a distributed 10% sample from the 2021 physics326
run. The later v4.9.5 pass examines that 2021 sample alone with a diﬀerent lower support edge.327
Version 4.9.7 tests a new full-2016 lower-support prescription but does not freeze one, so no new328
observed or combined state follows. The engineering data share the baseline tracker used in the329
published prompt searches; the 2021 data were recorded with the upgraded tracker and positron330
hodoscope. Although the 2019 run used the same upgraded detector, no 2019 data enter these331
analyses.332
Table 2 summarizes the run periods, analyzed samples, search intervals, and GP ﬁt supports.333
A mass hypothesis is tested only inside the search interval, whereas the GP is trained on the wider334
support after removing the local 2. 25 σm exclusion.335

searches covered 19 MeV to 81 MeV in 2015 and 39 MeV to 179 MeV in 2016 [ 3, 4].
The later v4.9.5 study changes only the lower support edge for a 2021-only extraction: it uses336
36–300 MeV while retaining the 50–250 MeV search interval. The accepted v4.2 three-campaign337
conﬁguration in Table 2 continues to use 40–300 MeV for 2021. Version 4.9.7 tests 28–33 MeV338
lower edges for full-2016 inference, with 34 MeV as a geometry control, but no edge passes its339
frozen Phase-1 rule. Table 12 records that stopped validation state.340
Integrated luminosity and analyzed fraction do not enter the GP interpolation. They enter later,341
with the target, acceptance, eﬃciency, and radiative fraction, when an extracted prompt yield is342
converted to an equivalent ϵ2 limit.343
The 2015 and 2016 samples are fully unblinded. The 2021 input is distributed across the run344
rather than drawn from a contiguous block, so it samples varied run conditions while leaving most345
of the exposure unopened. The ROOT ﬁle does not contain enough exposure metadata to infer346
an integrated luminosity; “10%” describes how the sample was constructed. The three-campaign347
result combines the full 2015 and 2016 samples with this 2021 sample and is not the ﬁnal result for348
the 2021 run.349
Figure 6 compares the unit-normalized invariant-mass shapes. Figure 7 shows the same spectra350
in events per MeV and marks the v4.2 ﬁt supports and search intervals.351

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
Figure 6: Unit-normalized invariant-mass shapes for the full 2015 and 2016 samples and the native
2021 10% sample. Each histogram is divided by its event count and bin width, so the comparison
shows spectral shape rather than sample size.
Figure 7: Invariant-mass distributions for the three inputs in the accepted v4.2 three-campaign
conﬁguration. Gray shading marks the GP ﬁt support and gold shading marks the narrower
interval in which signal hypotheses are tested. The mass-dependent 2. 25 σm exclusion is removed
from the GP support separately at every tested mass.
3 Event selection, mass resolution, and radiative fraction352
Each HPS input is an invariant-mass spectrum formed after a run-speciﬁc event selection. The353
selection ﬁxes the composition of the prompt e+e− sample, while the detector response sets the354
signal width and the radiative fraction sets its normalization. This section describes those three355
ingredients together.356
21

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
3.1 Prompt event selection357
Across the three HPS campaigns, the prompt selection follows the same experimental logic: oppo-358
sitely charged tracks matched to ECal clusters, tight cluster-time coincidence, track-quality and359
vertex-reconstruction requirements, and momentum cuts that retain prompt radiative tridents while360
suppressing accidentals and converted wide-angle bremsstrahlung. The 2015 and 2016 selections361
are inherited from the published prompt searches. For 2021, Table 3 distinguishes the upgraded-362
detector preselection in the full analysis note from the v16 prompt-TC production used for the363
native 10% v4.9.5 source. The production record establishes psum > 2. 8 GeV and no additional364
vertex-ﬁt χ2 requirement. It stores a three-dimensional hit count but does not establish the previ-365
ously quoted eight-hit two-dimensional electron requirement, so no such requirement is imposed in366
this description.367

published and internal engineering-run summaries. For 2021, the baseline entries follow the full-
analysis-note preselection [30, Sec. 4.8, Table 38 and App. A]. The prompt entries list only additional
requirements established for the v16 prompt-TC production used for the native 10% v4.9.5 source.

3.2 2015 mass resolution380
The 2015 signal width is calibrated with Møller data and propagated across mass with simulated381
A′ samples [ 31]. The Møller peak ﬁxes the detector resolution at a known mass, and the data–382
MC momentum-resolution diﬀerence determines the additional smearing applied to the simulation.383
Rescaling the ﬁtted A′ widths to that data anchor gives384
σ2015(m) = −9. 22 × 10− 5 + 5. 322 × 10− 2 m, (3)
with m and σ2015 expressed in GeV.385
The Møller measurement supplies the absolute response, while the simulated mass grid supplies386
its mass dependence. The associated uncertainty therefore reﬂects the Møller calibration, the target387
position, and the residual momentum-response mismodelling [ 3, 31]. At each test mass this curve388
sets both the Gaussian signal width and the width of the excluded training interval.389

Figure 13: 2015 mass-resolution parameterization from scaled A′ MC and Møller data [ 31]. The
Møller point anchors the absolute detector response in data, while the scaled A′simulation supplies
the mass dependence across the search window. Because the prompt dark-photon width is negligible,
this curve directly sets the resonance width assumed in the search and therefore the local background
entering the limit.
3.3 2016 mass resolution390
The 2016 calibration follows the same strategy over a broader mass range [ 28, 4]. Møller events
ﬁx the absolute response at 48. 5 MeV; full-energy-electron momentum peaks determine the extra
smearing needed to match data; and smeared A′ samples carry that calibration across the search
range. A fourth-order polynomial ﬁt to those samples deﬁnes the 2016 signal model,
σ2016(m) = 3 . 8 × 10− 4 + 4. 1 × 10− 2m − 2. 7 × 10− 1m2
+ 3. 49 m3 − 11. 11 m4, m ≤ 0. 18 GeV, (4)
Earlier extended-range studies continued the polynomial linearly above m0 = 0. 18 GeV with slope391
0. 0239 to avoid its high-mass turnover. The v4 2016 scan ends at 180 MeV, close to the published392
39 MeV to 179 MeV interval, so that continuation is not used. The 2015 scan similarly remains near393
its published 19 MeV to 81 MeV interval, with only the upper endpoint extended to 90 MeV.394
29

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
The smearing correction reconciles the Møller and simulated A′widths with the observed track-395
ing response [ 28, 4]. Because the resolution worsens with mass, both the signal template and the396
excluded training window broaden, increasing the prompt background beneath a narrow signal397
hypothesis.398
Figure 14: 2016 smeared- A′ and Møller-based mass-resolution summary [ 28]. The smeared A′
points show how the data-driven momentum-smearing correction is propagated away from the
single Møller calibration point. The worsening resolution with mass broadens the signal window
and is one of the main detector eﬀects limiting prompt sensitivity at higher mass.

3.5 Radiative fraction and the ε2 normalization411
The radiative fraction encodes the fraction of the selected prompt background arising from the412
purely radiative trident process,413
frad,d (m) = dNγ∗,d /dm
dNtri,d /dm + dNwab,d /dm , (7)
where the numerator is the selected radiative-trident component and the denominator is the full414
selected prompt background for dataset d [3, 4]. Because heavy-photon production is normalized415
to the radiative trident rate, frad is the physics quantity that converts a ﬁtted prompt signal yield416
32

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
into a limit on the coupling. With this notation,417
Kd(m) ≡ Ad(ε2 = 1) = 3π m frad,d (m) ρd(m)
2αEM
, A d = Kd(m) ε2, (8)
where ρd(m) is the selected prompt-spectrum density in dataset d. The engineering-run inputs418
follow the corresponding prompt searches, while the 2021 normalization remains provisional for the419
native 10% sample.420
3.5.1 2015 radiative fraction421
The 2015 prompt search used a constant approximation,422
frad, 2015(m) = 0 . 085, (9)
which the analysis retains as its 2015 baseline. The dedicated 2015 radiative-fraction study com-423
pares the selected radiative-trident component with the total prompt background and adopts the424
red horizontal line at 8.5% as a conservative description across the search interval. The constant425
comes from the internal HPS normalization study rather than from the GP ﬁt and is retained for426
consistency with the published 2015 analysis.427
Figure 18: Dedicated 2015 radiative-fraction inputs. The left panel shows the selected cross-section
composition, while the right panel shows the radiative fraction used for normalization. The red hor-
izontal line denotes the 8.5% constant adopted as the conservative ﬁt across the 2015 prompt-search
dataset. This quantity converts the measured prompt spectrum into the expected A′normalization,
so it aﬀects the ﬁnal ε2 interpretation rather than the local spectral ﬁt itself.
3.5.2 2016 radiative fraction428
The 2016 prompt search used a ﬁfth-order polynomial derived from radiative, trident, and converted-429
W AB MC after the full prompt selection. Those components determine the mass-dependent con-430
version from ﬁtted signal yield to ε2 over the 2016 search range. For the 2016 input, we instead431
use the constant approximation432
frad, 2016(m) = 0 . 05, (10)
33

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
which is numerically stable over the wider scan range. Figure 19 shows the mass-dependent simu-433
lation used in the published determination; the constant used here is a working numerical approxi-434
mation to that richer model.435
Figure 19: 2016 radiative-fraction study from radiative, trident, and converted-W AB MC [ 28]. The
simulation gives an intrinsically mass-dependent normalization, although this analysis uses the
constant frad, 2016 = 0. 05 for numerical stability.
3.5.3 2021 radiative fraction436
For the native 2021 10% sample, the radiative-fraction input remains provisional. We use437
frad, 2021(m) = 0 . 05. (11)
The 2021 full analysis note constructs the ratio from selected radiative-trident, tritrig, and W AB438
samples after preselection and a high-momentum requirement quoted as pvtx > 2. 8 GeV. A ﬁt over439
0. 05 < m < 0. 25 GeV gives the constant average f const
rad = 0. 0496 [30, Sec. 6.1, Figs. 92–93, Eqs. 25–440
26]. Thus 0.05 is a reasonable approximation over most of the 2021 search range, but it is not frozen441
for the ﬁnal unblinded result. That result will require a direct check of the normalization category442
against the v16 prompt-TC psum selection and its threshold convention, with the mass-dependent443
parameterization retained as a cross-check.444

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
Figure 20: 2021 selected-mass distributions used to form the radiative fraction in the full-analysis
note [ 30]. The upper panel shows the selected radiative, tritrig, and W AB components, while the

Figure 21: Fifth-order 2021 full-analysis-note ﬁt to the binned radiative fraction [ 30]. The ﬁtted

3.6 Systematic penalties from the radiative fraction445
The analysis applies a data-set-dependent radiative-fraction penalty,446
f eﬀ
rad,d (m) = (1 − δf,d ) frad,d (m). (12)
35

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
The penalty enters only when signal yield is converted to ε2. It changes the normalization of the447
inferred coupling without altering the blind-window likelihood.448
The v4 conﬁguration uses449
(δf, 2015, δ f, 2016, δ f, 2021) = (0 . 070, 0. 070, 0. 046). (13)
For a ﬁxed signal-yield limit, these shifts weaken the coupling-squared limit by factors 1/ 0. 93450
for 2015 and 2016 and 1/ 0. 954 for 2021. In the combined ﬁt each Kd(m) is reduced by its own451
amount, so a larger shared ε2 is required to produce the same yields. The factors are ﬁxed, one-452
sided normalization shifts; they do not enter the GP interpolation and are not proﬁled as nuisance453
parameters.454
The 7% engineering-run penalties reﬂect the several-percent uncertainty in the 2015 radiative455
and total e+e− composition and the corresponding 2016 uncertainty from the selected radiative,456
trident, and converted-W AB inputs [ 31, 4]. We retain 7% as a conservative ﬁrst-order treatment457
for both engineering runs. The 2021 term uses the upgraded-detector estimate below.458
The 2021 full analysis note derives a direct estimate from the generated RAD and W AB cross-459
section distributions shown in Fig. 22; propagating the quoted RAD and W AB cross-section widths460
with the relative normalization R = 0 . 244 gives a bounded fractional uncertainty of 3.5% [ 30,461
Sec. 9.1, Fig. 161]. The same note assigns an additional 3% preselection/radiative-fraction modeling462
uncertainty, motivated by W AB-selective mismodeling in the selected background [ 30, Sec. 9.2].463
Treating these two direct radiative-fraction normalization terms as independent gives464
δnote

(0. 035)2 + (0. 030)2 = 0. 046, f eﬀ

The resulting one-sided 2021 penalty is 4.6%, corresponding to a multiplicative factor of 0.954. The465
2015 and 2016 terms remain at 7%.466

4 Methodology471
4.1 From piecewise windows to ﬁxed-support GP ﬁts472
The original HPS GPR studies explored piecewise mass windows, redeﬁned the sidebands at each473
hypothesis, and considered several background proﬁles. They established that a GP could interpo-474
late the smooth trident spectrum, but the window-by-window ﬁts also produced less smooth limit475
curves, increased sensitivity to local ﬁt boundaries, and made a coherent multi-dataset combina-476
tion diﬃcult. The present workﬂow instead ﬁts the GP separately at each hypothesis on a ﬁxed,477
dataset-speciﬁc support, excluding only a narrow mass-dependent training region around the test478
mass. The support extends beyond the signal-search interval, but need not include empty turn-on479
bins or the full ROOT-histogram axis. This construction gives a smoother background prediction480
that can be validated and used in a combined likelihood with a shared parameter of interest.481
37

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
4.2 Search interval and GP ﬁt support482
The search interval answers where a resonance hypothesis is tested. The GP ﬁt support answers483
which surrounding bins constrain the smooth background at that hypothesis. Using the same484
endpoints for both deﬁnitions leaves the ﬁrst and last test masses with a one-sided training sample,485
even though the input histogram contains usable data beyond the search. The accepted v4.2486
three-campaign analysis uses the established 19–90, 39–180, and 50–250 MeV search intervals, with487
14–135, 30–210, and 40–300 MeV ﬁt supports for 2015, 2016, and 2021, respectively. The separate488
v4.9.5 2021-only pass keeps the 50–250 MeV search interval and changes that dataset’s ﬁt support489
to 36–300 MeV.490
These endpoints were declared before the v4 observed scan. The 2015 domain and the upper491
2016 edge follow the earlier functional-form validation ﬁts. The 2016 lower edge is placed at492
30 MeV rather than the historical 35 MeV ﬁt edge because the exact rebin-ﬁve geometry matters:493
at a 39 MeV hypothesis the blind interval begins at 35.063 MeV, while the ﬁrst bin center from a494
35 MeV support is 35.125 MeV. A nominal 35–210 MeV support would therefore still contain no495
low-side training-bin center at the lower search endpoint. The adopted domains retain at least 12496
and 20 low-side training bins at the 2015 and 2016 lower endpoints, and at least 138 and 51 high-497
side training bins at their upper endpoints. For the accepted v4.2 geometry, the corresponding 2021498
counts are 9 and 55. The bin-count audit is applied to every active dataset and mass hypothesis,499
rather than inferred only from continuous support boundaries.500
For 2021, v4.9 distinguished the support used to generate counts from the support retained in501
a GP ﬁt. Counts were generated once over 30–300 MeV; paired extractions then used 30–300 and502
40–300 MeV supports. This same-toy construction isolated the eﬀect of the lower edge, but did not503
freeze a new analysis support.504
Version 4.9.5 tested lower edges at 32, 34, 36, 38, and 40 MeV, with 30 MeV retained as an505
ineligible control, on the same 100 archived native-10% conditional backgrounds. The tests used506
masses of 55, 60, 65, and 70 MeV and injected strengths of z = 0 , 2, 5. Under the original strict507
protocol, a candidate failed if any injected cell had |p| ≥ 0. 5 and its two-sided 90% Student- t508
interval excluded zero; no edge passed. The practical rule adopted after that phase required at509
least 9 of the 12 mass–strength means, including at least 3 of the 4 background-only means, to have510
|p|< 0. 75, with none at or above 1.25; the technical gates were unchanged. Both 36 and 38 MeV511
qualiﬁed. The declared minimax tie rule selected 36 MeV because its score lay within the 0.10512
tie margin and it retained more support. The continuation and the full 100-background summary513
passed without further retuning. This conditional result froze the 36–300 MeV support for the514
v4.9.5 2021-only observed pass; it is not a direct coverage result and does not revise the accepted515
v4.2 combined-analysis card. The four conﬁguration states are summarized in Table 4.516
Version 4.9.7 is a later, separately frozen attempt to qualify the lower support for full-2016517
inference. Its conditional Phase-1 ensemble rejects every eligible edge, so the declared stop rule518
prevents a new 2016 observed scan and three-campaign recombination. Section 5.12 gives that519
outcome; it is not a retrospective alteration of any state in Table 4.520

4.3 Observable, scan grid, and blinding521
The input observable is the reconstructed e+e− invariant-mass histogram for each dataset. Through-522
out this section, d labels the dataset, m denotes the reconstructed e+e− invariant mass, m0 is the523
test-mass hypothesis, and σd(m) is the dataset-speciﬁc mass resolution introduced in Section 3.524
The default scan step in the package is525
∆ m = 1 MeV, (15)
and the blind window for a mass hypothesis m0 is centered on the detector resolution,526
Bd(m0) = [ m0 − nblindσd(m0), m 0 + nblindσd(m0)] . (16)
The package exposes the extraction half-width and GP training exclusion as independent conﬁg-527
uration values. Earlier injection and GP-toy validation studies used nblind = 1 . 64 with the same528
1. 64 σd training exclusion. The current observed-limit and refmatched closure baseline documented529
later in this note uses a wider 2. 25 σd blind/extraction window with the GP training exclusion also530
set to 2. 25 σd. For an ideal Gaussian line shape, the 1. 64 σd interval contains531
f ideal
blind(1. 64) = erf
(1. 64√
2
)
≃ 0. 899, (17)
39

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
while the 2. 25 σd interval contains532
f ideal
blind(2. 25) = erf
(2. 25√
2
)
≃ 0. 976. (18)
This point matters conceptually and numerically: the current code does not renormalize the signal533
template inside either blind window. Instead, it keeps the full Gaussian normalization and lets only534
the blinded subset enter the local likelihood.535
The notation Bd(m0) denotes the set of histogram bins that lie inside the blinded interval for536
dataset d at test mass m0. In the current production conﬁguration there is no additional gap537
between the extraction window and the GP training sample: when the extraction and training-538
exclusion half-widths are equal, the training exclusion starts immediately outside Bd(m0). A wider539
exclusion, for example 1. 96 σd instead of 1. 64 σd, reduces signal leakage into the training region but540
also increases the interpolation distance and therefore the GP predictive uncertainty. In the simple541
background-dominated proxy S/
√
B ∝ F (k)/
√
k with F (k) = erf( k/
√
2), the optimum lies near542
k ≃ 1. 4, so the historical 1. 64 σd extraction was close to the pure-sensitivity optimum. Section 5543
then motivates the current 2. 25 σd blind/extraction choice as a signal-recovery improvement after544
full-reﬁt pseudoexperiments showed appreciable training-region absorption at narrower exclusions.545
The bin assignment is implemented with the ﬁxed histogram bin centers, not by splitting partly546
overlapping bins. Let ci = ( ei + ei+1)/ 2 be the center of bin i. The full bin is included in the547
blinded likelihood when548
m0 − nblindσd(m0) ≤ ci ≤ m0 + nblindσd(m0), (19)
and the full bin is included in the GP sideband training sample only when its center lies strictly549
outside the training-exclusion interval. Thus a bin whose edge overlaps the blind boundary is550
treated by its center: if the center is inside the blind interval, the entire bin is blinded and enters551
the local likelihood; if the center is outside, the bin remains available for training unless it is removed552
by a wider training-exclusion interval. No fractional observed count is assigned to both regions.553
This same center mask is used consistently for the observed counts, the GP prediction points, the554
covariance slice, and the blinded-bin signal-template slice.555
The ﬁnite binning is therefore part of the deﬁnition of the numerical result. The Gaussian556
signal template is integrated over the full bin edges in Eq. ( 35), but the selected set of bins changes557
discretely when a boundary passes a bin center. Consequently the quoted local signiﬁcance and ε2558
limit are not meaningful to a precision ﬁner than the mass binning and scan grid used to deﬁne these559
masks. The production toys and validation plots use the same binning convention, so this edge-bin560
eﬀect is accounted for by applying the identical discrete procedure to data, pseudoexperiments,561
background predictions, signal templates, and yield-to-coupling conversions.562
4.4 Log-space preprocessing and the choice α = 1/y563
Following recent HEP GPR background-modeling studies [ 32, 26], the package ﬁts the GP in564
transformed coordinates:565
x = log m, z =
{
log y, y > 0,
0, y = 0, (20)
where y is the observed bin count. The log transformation is used for two related reasons. First,566
the prompt trident spectrum spans a wide dynamic range, and modeling z rather than y turns567
multiplicative shape variations into approximately additive ones. Second, x = log m makes equal568
distances in the GP coordinate correspond to equal fractional changes in mass, which is the relevant569
40

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
scale once the detector resolution is written in the transformed coordinate as σx(m) ≃ σd(m)/m .570
Barr and Liu emphasize that applying logarithms to both axes improves convergence and perfor-571
mance for wide, steeply falling spectra [ 32]. The GP regression step uses scikit-learn’s GaussianPro-572
cessRegressor estimator [ 33]; the surrounding HPS analysis code supplies the histogram handling,573
mass-dependent sideband masks, signal templates, proﬁle likelihood, and CL s limit construction.574
The GP is therefore used to model an unobserved smooth mean function ztrue,d (x) for the log-575
count spectrum. In that language, “latent” means only that the smooth log-rate is not observed576
directly; it is inferred from the histogram counts through the transformed coordinates and the het-577
eroscedastic variance term. The GP mean vector is the vector of latent log-count means evaluated578
at the selected training-bin coordinate. For Poisson counts Yi ∼ Pois(λi), the delta method gives579
Var[log Yi] ≈ Var[Yi]/E [Yi]2 ≈ 1/λ i, which motivates the plug-in choice αi ≈ 1/y i for occupied bins.580
A heteroscedastic diagonal term αi is then added to the training covariance,581
αi =
{
1/y i, y i > 0,
1, y i = 0, (21)
582
Kobs = K(X, X ) + diag(α), (22)
so that each αi plays the role of an observation-variance term for bin i. This is the quantity that583
GaussianProcessRegressor adds to the diagonal during ﬁtting, so it is treated here as a variance584
model rather than an arbitrary tuning parameter.585
This is a deliberate departure from the earlier Barr–Liu-inspired draft parameterization. In586
the present analysis, αi is treated as the latent-space variance assignment associated with the587
transformed counts, while avoiding the extra log y weighting explored in the ﬁrst rough drafts. For588
empty bins the adopted ﬂoor αi = 1 is a unit variance in log-count space, not a pseudo-count of one589
event. It simply prevents zero-count bins from receiving ill-deﬁned or disproportionate weight after590
the log transformation. In this high-statistics search such bins are rare, so the ﬂoor acts mainly as591
a conservative legacy-stable safeguard rather than as a dominant source of uncertainty.592
The distinction from Ref. [ 32] is more speciﬁc than a diﬀerence in kernel choice. Barr and Liu593
also use a Constant ×RBF kernel and optimize its amplitude and length scale. Their independent594
methodological study uses a spectrum derived from a published CMS search; it is not a CMS595
Collaboration GPR result. In addition to optimizing the kernel, they treat the diagonal array as596
tunable regularization. For occupied bins their nominal prescription is597
αBL
i =
√ yi
yi log yi
= 1√ yi log yi
, (23)
and ﬁxed regional multipliers fi are then applied to αBL
i . Reducing the ﬁrst eleven multipliers to 0.1598
raised the fraction of 100 background-only pseudoexperiments passing their BumpHunter criterion599
from 51% to 87%, but localized residual structures remained; their high-luminosity benchmark sub-600
sequently extended the reduced-multiplier region to the ﬁrst twenty bins [ 32]. The study therefore601
demonstrates that diagonal tuning can improve a particular benchmark, while also showing that602
the tuned region is sample- and regime-dependent.603
The active HPS conﬁguration applies no regional multiplier to the early bins and ﬁxes Eq. ( 21) as604
the delta-method variance model. This choice matters because scikit-learn’s alpha parameter has605
both an observation-noise interpretation and a numerical Tikhonov-regularization interpretation606
[34]. HPS adopts the former and places its separately validated smoothness control in the allowed607
length-scale domain. These are complementary controls: changing αi changes the leverage of bin608
i on the covariance diagonal, whereas changing ℓ changes the oﬀ-diagonal correlation range of the609
latent function. No matched study presented here establishes that one strategy is intrinsically610
superior to the other.611

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
4.5 Kernel choice and distinct regularization roles612
The baseline kernel in the current package is a single ConstantKernel ×RBF model,613
k(x, x ′) = C exp

with broad amplitude bounds and a resolution-scaled RBF length-scale domain. This is the same614
simple kernel class used in the methodological studies of Refs. [ 26, 32], but the surrounding noise615
and validation choices need not be the same. The two quantities optimized in every HPS sideband616
ﬁt are C and ℓ. The ConstantKernel value C = k(x, x ) is the pointwise prior variance of the latent617
log-rate, so
√
C is its prior standard deviation; C is not a ﬁtted event-count normalization. The618
length scale ℓ controls how quickly covariance falls with separation in x = log m, and therefore how619
rapidly the background function may bend. The ﬁxed αi values are not a third kernel parameter620
and there is no optimized WhiteKernel in the baseline. They enter only through the observation-621
covariance diagonal.622
Figure 25 separates these roles graphically. This separation is useful when discussing regulariza-623

and smoothness of the latent function, and changing αi changes how strongly one observation625

4.6 Resolution-scaled length-scale parameterization and bounds631
Because the GP is trained in x = log m, detector resolution must be translated into log-mass units.632
The package deﬁnes633
σx(m) = log( m + σd(m)) − log m ≃ σd(m)
m (σd ≪ m), (25)
and uses this local physical scale to set the RBF bounds. Under the local, resolution-scaled policy,634
ℓmin,d (m) = kmin,d σx(m), ℓ max,d (m) = kmax,d σx(m). (26)
For the RBF covariance used here,635
kRBF(x, x ′) = σ2
f exp
[
− (x − x′)2
2ℓ2
]
, kRBF(x, x + ℓ)
kRBF(x, x ) = e− 1/ 2 ≃ 0. 61. (27)
Thus ℓ is a covariance-decay scale in the GP input coordinate: larger values favor smoother back-636
ground functions, while smaller values admit more rapid variation [ 24, 25]. The ratio ℓ/σ x(m)637
expresses that nuisance-model smoothness relative to the width of the narrow signal hypothesis.638
It is not, without an independently validated mapping, a detector resolution measurement or a639
physical background correlation length. The ﬁtted ℓopt is the log-marginal-likelihood optimum640
conditional on the kernel, data support, sideband mask, noise model, and conﬁgured optimizer641
domain. Consequently an optimum at ℓmax establishes only that the numerical constraint is active;642
it neither measures a physical scale at the boundary nor locates the unconstrained optimum. The643
implementation optimizes log-transformed kernel hyperparameters within these conﬁgured bounds644
[34].645
For a ﬁxed dataset d and mass hypothesis m0, let zsb contain only the transformed sideband646
observations and deﬁne647
Ky(C, ℓ) = C Rℓ(Xsb, X sb) + diag(αsb). (28)
The optimized pair maximizes648
log p(zsb |Xsb, C, ℓ ) = − 1
2 zT
sbK− 1
y zsb − 1
2 log |Ky| −nsb
2 log(2π). (29)
The quadratic term rewards agreement with the sideband observations, while the determinant term649
accounts for the covariance volume used to obtain that agreement. The implementation searches in650
the logarithms of C and ℓ with multiple optimizer restarts. This is an empirical-Bayes, or type-II651
maximum-likelihood, selection of the covariance parameters; it is not a proﬁle of ℓ as a frequentist652
nuisance parameter in the subsequent local CL s likelihood.653
Figure 26 shows why the optimum is speciﬁc to a dataset and mass hypothesis. Moving m0654
changes which bins are excluded from training and changes the resolution-scaled domain through655
σx(m0). The same dataset can therefore return a diﬀerent (Copt, ℓ opt) at neighboring hypotheses656
even though the kernel family is unchanged. An interior maximum means only that the conﬁgured657
range is nonbinding for that ﬁt. A maximum on a boundary means that the range is active, while a658
long shallow likelihood ridge means that the parameters are weakly identiﬁed. None of these cases659
turns ℓopt into a direct measurement of detector resolution or a physical background correlation660
length.661

Figure 26: Meaning of per-mass-hypothesis hyperparameter optimization. Panel (a) shows one
schematic sideband problem in u = (x − x0)/σ x(m0). The shaded 2. 25 σx interval is excluded from
training; the three curves illustrate the diﬀerent interpolation behavior allowed by short, interior,
and long candidate length scales. Panel (b) shows the joint log-marginal-likelihood surface for the
same synthetic sidebands. The star is the bounded maximum in (C, ℓ), and the elongated contours
expose their possible tradeoﬀ. Panel (c) shows that moving m0 changes both the sideband mask and
the local resolution scale that deﬁnes the allowed ℓ interval. Panel (d) illustrates how a scan records
an interior optimum diﬀerently from repeated upper-bound contact: the former is a conditional
best ﬁt within a nonbinding range, whereas the latter diagnoses an active numerical domain and
motivates a predeclared range and closure study. The ﬁgure is explanatory only; it contains no
HPS observations or ﬁt results.
This distinction determines how the hyperparameter study enters a physics result. If the allowed662
range forces ℓ too low, the GP retains unnecessary signal-scale ﬂexibility and can absorb part of663
a localized excess; if the ﬁtted background is forced too smooth, broad mismodeling can instead664
leave a biased local residual. Raising only the upper bound permits, but does not require, a665
smoother solution. A larger data set also need not prefer a larger ℓ: additional statistics can666
resolve background structure and move the optimum downward. The upper range is therefore667
selected from boundary occupancy, likelihood ordering, optimizer stability, and ﬁxed-amplitude668
44

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
signal-recovery diagnostics, never from a favorable observed limit or local p0. These checks can669
justify a bounded candidate range, but they do not by themselves establish frequentist coverage.670
The v4.2 result uses the dataset-speciﬁc factors in Table 5. The lower factors retain their earlier671
full-reﬁt closure support; changing the 2016 upper factor does not requalify those closure studies672
at full statistics.673

Figure 27 summarizes the same construction in the language used by the implemented likelihood.708
The sideband bins set the log-count training sample and its heteroscedastic bin-noise assignment,709
the kernel hyperparameters are optimized only on those sidebands, and the trained GP is then710
conditioned to predict the mean and covariance in the blinded mass window. That predicted count-711
space mean and covariance are the background inputs passed to the local proﬁled signal extraction.712

in the blind window supplies the background mean and covariance that enter the local likelihood;

4.7 Blind-window prediction in count space713
For each mass point, the package trains the GP only on the bins outside the training exclusion
window and predicts the posterior mean and covariance of the latent smooth log-rate in the blinded
bins. Since the ﬁt is performed in log-count space, that posterior is mapped back to count space
through the standard lognormal moment relations:
µi = exp

µ(log)

Cov(λi, λ j) = µiµj

46

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
The resulting blind-window mean vector bd(m) and covariance matrix Cd(m) are the central sta-714
tistical objects entering both the local extraction ﬁt and the CL s limit. In other words, once the715
GP prediction has been translated back into count space, the analysis no longer works with an716
abstract regression model; it works with an explicit multivariate background prediction for the717
latent expected counts in the blinded bins. The Poisson counting ﬂuctuation of the observed nd,i is718
handled separately by the likelihood, so Cd should be read as the covariance of the GP-predicted719
background mean rather than the covariance of the observed histogram counts themselves.720
To propagate that correlated GP uncertainty into the proﬁle likelihood, the analysis uses the721
Cholesky factorization722
Cd(m) = Ld(m)L⊤
d (m), (32)
with Ld(m) the lower-triangular Cholesky factor of the positive-semideﬁnite blind-window covari-723
ance. Introducing an auxiliary standard-normal nuisance vector θd ∼ N (0, I ) and the background-724
deformation ﬁeld ∆ bd(m) = Ld(m)θd then gives ∆ bd ∼ N (0, Cd). This is the standard HEP725
construction for encoding correlated Gaussian-constrained nuisance variations inside a proﬁle like-726
lihood model [ 35, 36]. In the present analysis it is especially important because the GP prediction727
induces nontrivial bin-to-bin correlations across the blinded window; carrying those correlations728
through Ldθd lets the local signiﬁcance and CL s limit respond to coherent background deforma-729
tions rather than misinterpreting the GP uncertainty as independent per-bin noise.730

4.10 Asymptotic and toy calibration812
The local-signiﬁcance mapping introduced above is treated asymptotically throughout the produc-813
tion scan. For upper limits, the same bounded statistic ˜qAd is calibrated in one of two ways. In814
asymptotic mode, the background-only Asimov data set is used to evaluate the reference quantity815
˜qAd,A , so that816

In toy mode, the same statistic ˜qAd is calibrated directly with background-only and signal-plus-817
background pseudoexperiments rather than by switching to a diﬀerent ordering variable. The818
shared-coupling combination described below uses the identical logic after replacing the single-819
dataset amplitude Ad with the common parameter of interest ε2.820
The pseudoexperiment program remains important even with the asymptotic formulae in place821
because the HPS likelihood combines a multi-bin Poisson term, a GP-conditioned blind-window822
covariance, a non-renormalized Gaussian signal template, and a mass-dependent yield-to- ε2 conver-823
sion. The pull, spurious-signal, and injection-recovery diagnostics of Section 5 test this machinery824
under the declared conditional backgrounds. They are not direct CL s coverage studies or a scan-825
wide calibration of Z.826

4.11 Matched-reference injection–extraction diagnostics827
The v4.5 contract deﬁnes an injection axis through a background-only reference ﬁt that uses the828
same support, binning, exclusion mask, kernel bounds, preprocessing, and GP-reﬁt path as the829
corresponding toy extraction. If F[n; w] denotes that full procedure for counts n and the blind-830
window slice of the unit-normalized full-range signal template w, then831
( ˆAref , σ A, ref ) = F[nref ; w], A inj(z) = z σA, ref , z ∈ {0, 1, 3, 5}. (52)
The reference uncertainty is a ﬁxed calibration of the injection coordinate at a given source, toy832
index, and mass. It is not the post-injection uncertainty returned for an individual extraction.833
For accepted state t, the signed pull and paired response are
pt(z) =
ˆAt(z) − Ainj,t (z)
σA,t (z) , (53)
Rt(z) =
ˆAt(z) − ˆAt(0)
Ainj,t (z) , z > 0. (54)
The zero-injection mean pull diagnoses a conditional spurious-signal oﬀset under the declared gener-834
ating truth. Its width probes the returned uncertainty scale, while Rt separates injection response835
from a background-speciﬁc zero-signal oﬀset. These quantities use the toy-level σA,t in the denomi-836
nator. A ﬁnite 25-background sample supports two-sided 90% Student- t intervals on the mean and837
descriptive widths, not a direct coverage statement.838
Full reﬁtting and no-reﬁt displays answer diﬀerent questions. Holding a GP state ﬁxed isolates839
the local proﬁle-likelihood extraction. Reﬁtting the GP on every pseudo-spectrum additionally tests840
background adaptation and possible signal absorption. Version 4.9 uses the latter matched-reﬁt841
path. Optimizer acceptance is decided from ﬁt state, likelihood, reproducibility, covariance, and842
kernel-coordinate diagnostics before amplitudes or pulls are summarized; no row is selected because843
its pull is favorable.844

These quantities are therefore limit diagnostics rather than discovery-signiﬁcance measures in853
the sense of p0. If the observed limit lies near the median of the background-only ensemble, then854
roughly half of the toys lie above it and half lie below it, making both pstrong and pweak naturally855
O(0. 5). Their ﬁnite resolution is 1/N toys. In the v4.2 combined ensemble, Ntoys = 300 , so a856
one-count change is 1/ 300 ≃ 0. 0033 and a zero-count tail is reported as 0 of 300 rather than as857
an exact zero probability. These toy-limit diagnostics cannot by themselves establish or refute a858
high-signiﬁcance local excess in the discovery statistic deﬁned by the one-sided local p0 mapping859
above.860

For the present result, Eq. ( 73) supplies a separate analytic look-elsewhere reference for the local895

4.15 Signal absorption and alternative background-proﬁle treatments902
The production baseline documented here is the additive Gaussian-proﬁle treatment of blind-903
window background uncertainty. That choice is natural because the GP delivers an additive mean904
vector and covariance matrix in the blinded bins, and the entire current toy-validation program is905
built around that object. At the same time, the remaining signal-absorption question should be906
stated explicitly. The newly adopted leakage-retaining signal model removes one obvious source907
of bias associated with blind-window renormalization, but it does not by itself prove that the full908
procedural toy workﬂow is free of absorption once the GP is reﬁt toy by toy.909
The most likely residual sources are the reﬁtting freedom of the GP hyperparameters, especially910
the eﬀective length scale, and the ﬂexibility of the Gaussian-proﬁle background nuisance itself. For911
54

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
that reason, the current note keeps the Gaussian-proﬁle model as the mainline baseline while912
reserving ﬁxed-background and alternative Poisson-style treatments as targeted robustness studies.913
Those alternatives are not presented as equally mature production choices. They become necessary914
follow-up tests if production-faithful pseudoexperiments demonstrate that the current nuisance915
model absorbs an unacceptable fraction of injected signal.916
4.16 Dimuon-threshold interpretation917
The yield-to-coupling conversion in Eq. ( 40) is an electron-channel normalization: the ﬁtted reso-918
nance amplitude is the A′ → e+e− yield visible in the prompt invariant-mass spectrum. For the919
minimal kinetically mixed dark photon, this is also the full visible branching interpretation below920
the dimuon threshold, mA′ < 2mµ = 211 . 3167 MeV, where the µ+µ− ﬁnal state is kinematically921
closed. This is the same ﬁxed-target convention used in the published HPS prompt searches [ 2, 3, 4].922
Above threshold, the same electron-channel yield corresponds to a weaker minimal-model limit on923
ε2 because the total width includes the newly open A′→ µ+µ− mode [ 1, 41].924
Neglecting the electron mass and remaining below the two-pion hadronic threshold, the charged-925
lepton width ratio is926

The scan range used for a look-elsewhere statement must match the range of the quoted physics935

electron-channel scan is promoted to a 170 MeV to 250 MeV minimal-model result, the correspond-938

5 Background-model validation with full-reﬁt pseudoexperiments940
5.1 Validation question and scope941
The validation begins with a concrete local question. If a smooth, data-like spectrum contains no942
resonance, does the complete GPR sideband ﬁt return a spurious signal? If a known signal is added,943
does the same procedure recover it with an uncertainty of the right scale? The pseudoexperiments944
exercise the count-space prediction, the mass-dependent blind and training masks, the full-range945
Gaussian signal template, the matched-reference injection convention, and a fresh GP optimization946
for every pseudo-spectrum. The statistical deﬁnitions are given in Section 4.11; this section presents947
the physical motivation, the generating spectra, the ensembles, the numerical safeguards, and the948
closure evidence.949
Three distinctions are important throughout this section. First, a generating function is a950
smooth stress truth ﬁtted to an HPS source spectrum; it is not claimed to be the unique physical951
parameterization of the trident continuum. Second, closure is conditional on that speciﬁed truth952
and on the analysis card used for the extraction. It therefore tests the behavior of the procedure953
for data-like spectra without measuring a bias in the observed data. Third, the four exposure lanes954
are not interchangeable. The 1% × 10 and 1% × 100 spectra derive from the native 1% source,955
while native 10% and 10% × 10 derive from an independently selected 10% source. Cross-source956
diﬀerences include source and selection shape as well as normalization.957
5.2 Closure observables and decision rules958
At zero injection, the signed pull p = ˆA/ ˆσA is a spurious-signal statistic. The ensemble mean959
tests whether the extraction is centered and the sample standard deviation tests whether the960
returned uncertainty has the appropriate scale. At nonzero injection, the pull of Eq. ( 53) tests961
centering about the known amplitude and the paired response of Eq. ( 54) separates recovery of the962
56

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
Figure 31: Analysis and validation ﬂow. In the data path, sidebands around each mass hypothesis
train the GP and the blinded bins enter the signal-plus-background likelihood. In the validation
path, a smooth source-ﬁtted mean is Poisson ﬂuctuated, a known signal may be added, and the
complete GP ﬁt and extraction are repeated. The zero-injection ensemble tests spurious signal;
nonzero injections test signal recovery and uncertainty calibration. These are extraction-closure
tests, not conﬁdence-limit coverage or a scan-wide signiﬁcance calibration.
57

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
added signal from a background-speciﬁc zero-signal oﬀset. Strengths z = 0 , 1, 3, 5 reuse the same963
background pseudo-spectrum and are consequently correlated; they are one response curve, not964
four independent conﬁrmations.965
For each 100-background cell we report the usable count n, the pull mean and its two-sided 90%966
Student-t interval, and the pull width with its exact normal-theory 90% chi-square interval. A cell967
is reported only if at least 95 of its 100 ﬁts pass the numerical criteria. We account for multiplicity968
in two ways: a Holm correction over the two planned replacement points at z = 0, and a separate969
Holm correction over the twenty displayed four-lane, ﬁve-mass points at z = 0 after substitution.970
The correlated nonzero injections are response diagnostics and do not enter either family. We ﬂag971
a mean only when its Holm-adjusted two-sided p value is below 0.05 and |p| ≥0. 2. The absence of972
such a ﬂag is not proof that the bias is zero.973
A practical centering tolerance, |p|< 0. 5, was chosen after the 100-background results were974
available. It is therefore a descriptive criterion, not a predeclared equivalence test. We report it975
alongside the t/Holm results, which are more sensitive to small but statistically resolved oﬀsets.976
For the two replacement points we also ask whether the full 90% interval on the mean lies inside977
the practical band. A pull width is called consistent with one only when its 90% interval contains978
one. None of these tests measures CL s coverage.979
5.3 Training exclusion and blind-window choice980
The GP must not be trained on the core or near tails of the signal it is later asked to test. A Gaussian981
signal retains non-negligible weight outside a 1. 64σm central interval, so training immediately beside982
that interval can oﬀer signal-like counts to the background ﬁt and reduce the recovered amplitude.983
The training-exclusion study therefore varied the sideband boundary before any observed limit was984
used to choose it.985
Figure 32: Selected 2. 25σm blind and training-exclusion geometry. The GP is trained outside the
widened interval, while all bins inside it enter the local signal-plus-background likelihood. This
retains the signal tails for extraction without allowing those same bins to inﬂuence the sideband
ﬁt.

production outcome is a single, explicit mass-dependent geometry for the background and signal989
ﬁts.990
5.4 Generating spectra and the role of functional forms991
The principal background toys are generated independently of the GP used for their extraction. A992
positive analytic intensity is ﬁtted to a native HPS mass spectrum, normalized on the speciﬁed toy993
support, integrated over every histogram bin, and Poisson ﬂuctuated. A complete signal template is994
then added when z > 0. Every pseudo-spectrum is subsequently analyzed with a new GP sideband995
ﬁt. This analytic truth/full-reﬁt construction is the central background-model test because success996
is not guaranteed by generating toys from the GP model that will later ﬁt them.997
The earlier 2015 and 2016 studies used independently ﬁtted shifted sigmoid–power–exponential998
families. The initial 2021 catalogue selected an unshifted sigmoid–power–exponential model (inter-999
nal label fSigPowExpQ) for the native 1% source. The later four-lane study used a generalized-1000
gamma threshold model (internal label fGenGammaThresh), ﬁtted independently to the native 1%1001
and native 10% sources. These choices span smooth threshold, power-law, and broad-tail curvature1002
without adding resonance-scale structure. Figure 33 shows the source spectra, selected means, and1003
Poisson-ensemble construction.1004
The mass-resolved injection scans selected the lower RBF length-scale factors without using1005
the observed limit curve. For the frozen settings, the aggregate zero-signal mean pulls are +0. 029,1006
−0. 016, and −0. 005 for the 2015, 2016 10%, and 2021 1% studies, respectively; the corresponding1007
nonzero-injection signed averages are −0. 223, −0. 262, and −0. 034. Mass-resolved mean, width,1008
signiﬁcance-residual, and recovery checks support these summaries. They motivate the chosen1009
lower factors but do not replace direct mass-by-mass closure.1010

2. 25σm blind and training exclusions, two required sidebands, and twelve randomized optimizer1015
restarts in addition to the nominal start. The original four lanes use the common generalized-1016
gamma threshold model ﬁtted independently to the native 1% and native 10% sources. Their1017

5.6 Targeted validation of the rising threshold1021
1% × 10 threshold-turn-on truth. The ﬁrst replacement is the source-ﬁtted model developed1022
in the dedicated near-threshold study. Over 35–80 MeV it is a logistic turn-on multiplied by an1023
exponentiated degree-ﬁve Chebyshev expansion and is joined smoothly to the speciﬁed high-mass1024
tail. The degree is selected before any GP extraction: the native-bin deviance per degree of freedom1025
is 0.994 for the native 1% source and the ﬁve-bin value is 0.850, with no parameter-bound contact.1026
Its disclosed 80–300 MeV tail Poisson deviance per degree of freedom is 1.122, so this selected1027
1%-source truth is broadly data-like over the complete 40–300 MeV support rather than only in1028
the ﬁtted turn-on interval. The earlier 20-background result at 65 MeV had mean pull 0.300 and1029
width 1.109; version 4.9.1 replaces that pilot with a predeclared 100-background continuation and1030
reports 90% intervals.1031
Native-10% 30–300 Me V support. The second replacement models the measured 30–40 MeV1032
shoulder and analyzes it with the complete 30–300 MeV GP support. Its threshold component is1033
a logistic turn-on times a degree-six exponentiated Chebyshev form ﬁtted over 30–80 MeV, joined1034
with a C2 taper to the frozen smooth anchor. In the native 10% source, the local native-bin1035
and ﬁve-bin deviances per degree of freedom are 1.140 and 1.421. Thus the generating spectrum1036
is data-like in the threshold-sensitive region even though the simple historical family is not. Its1037
85–300 MeV tail deviance per degree of freedom is 6.220; this native-10% lane therefore relies on1038
the explicit weak-correlation and tail-inﬂuence qualiﬁcation below rather than on a support-wide1039
goodness-of-ﬁt claim.1040

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
This study tests the 65 MeV extraction under the 30–300 MeV support considered in v4.9.1; it1041
does not determine the 36–300 MeV support adopted in v4.9.5. The question here is whether a1042
data-like generating mean that passes the local source-ﬁt tests can be processed by the complete1043
extraction without material signal bias. With log preprocessing, the direct RBF correlation between1044
masses m and m′ is1045
ρ(m, m ′) = exp
[

. (77)
At 65 MeV, the accepted optimized length scales in the two actual replacement ensembles span1046
0.2784–0.3185 for 1% × 10 and 0.2468–0.2691 for native 10%, with medians 0.2963 and 0.2592. The1047
corresponding direct correlations with 150 MeV span 1.10–3.19% and 0.321–0.800%, respectively.1048
Across the complete 55–70 MeV set the largest direct correlations with 150 MeV occur at 70 MeV1049
and are 5.71% and 1.81%. The correlation decreases rapidly above 150 MeV. These results quantify1050
why a threshold-focused source ﬁt can provide a meaningful local test while the generated spectrum1051
and extraction still retain the full GP support.1052
The correlation calculation is a ﬁxed-hyperparameter statement, not a claim that high-mass1053
bins have mathematically zero inﬂuence. All retained bins still enter the global marginal likelihood1054
and can shift the optimized length scale or kernel constant. A same-input diagnostic makes the1055
aggregate eﬀect explicit: after ﬁxing the selected hyperparameters, removing the training bins above1056
150 MeV shifts the 65 MeV pull by a mean of −0. 130 with a paired 90% interval [−0. 151, −0. 110] in1057
1%× 10, and by −0. 195 [−0. 211, −0. 179] in native 10%. The maximum absolute shifts are 0.376 and1058
0.400. In the ﬁrst lane the mean changes from 0.139 to 0.009; in the second it changes from −0. 2461059
to −0. 441, whose 90% interval [−0. 611, −0. 271] crosses the adopted practical boundary. Counts1060
above 150 MeV therefore have modest, statistically resolved collective inﬂuence even though every1061
pairwise RBF correlation is small. This diagnostic holds the chosen hyperparameters ﬁxed and1062
does not test their global-likelihood response to tail removal.1063
The optimized kernels nevertheless explain why a threshold-focused source ﬁt is scientiﬁcally1064
informative for the complete card: the low-mass extraction is directly coupled most strongly to the1065
turn-on region, while the generated spectrum, speciﬁed smooth continuation, and full extraction1066
retain the actual support. The restricted source ﬁt thus provides a targeted conditional validation1067
of local 65 MeV behavior under that full-card construction. It does not establish tail-independence1068
or universal GP closure for every possible high-mass truth.1069
5.7 Matched reﬁts and numerical selection1070
For every background, mass, and scheme, an independently optimized background-only ﬁt supplies1071
the matched reference uncertainty. Signals with z = 1 , 3, 5 are generated with mean amplitude1072
Ainj = zσA, ref , their full Gaussian templates are added to the same background counts, and the GP1073
is reﬁtted after applying the same training mask. The injected amplitude is therefore calibrated to1074
the local background-only ﬁt, whereas the pull denominator is always the uncertainty returned by1075
the injected ﬁt.1076
Whether a ﬁt is kept never depends on its ﬁtted signal or pull. Repeated attempts use the same1077
pseudo-data with independent optimizer starts. We choose among them using the GP log marginal1078
likelihood, reproducibility of the length scale and kernel constant, covariance validity, uncertainty-1079
ratio checks, and contact with the kernel bounds. The choice never uses ˆA, the pull, agreement1080
with the injected value, ε2, or a local p value. A statistical outlier is retained whenever it passes1081
these numerical checks.1082
The historical study attempted 8000 ﬁts and retained 7994; every cell contains 99 or 100 usable1083
ﬁts. The six rejected ﬁts failed reproducibility checks. Each new continuation contains 400 usable1084
62

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
ﬁts—100 backgrounds at each of z = 0 , 1, 3, 5—with no rejection and no kernel-bound contact.1085
Reaching those solutions required 614 optimizer attempts in the 1% × 10 threshold-turn-on lane1086
and 610 in the native 10% lane. Failed starts remain recorded, and checks of the covariance, counts,1087
and pull calculation agree. Numerical selection is therefore independent of the physics result.1088

5.9 Recovery of injected signals1123
A centered zero-signal ensemble is necessary but not suﬃcient: a background model could remove1124
the false oﬀset and still absorb a real narrow signal. The nonzero rows therefore repeat the full GP1125
ﬁt after adding matched one-, three-, and ﬁve-reference-sigma signals. Figure 66 displays the mean1126
and width at every strength and mass; Table 9 gives the numerical 65 MeV replacement results,1127
including the paired response R = ( ˆAz − ˆA0)/A inj.1128
For the historical four-lane suite, the paired response separates signal recovery from the ad-1129
ditive background oﬀset. In the two replacements, the mean response is stable at 0.966–0.9691130
over z = 1 , 3, 5, with mutually consistent 90% intervals and no worsening with strength. The full1131
procedure therefore recovers 96.6–96.9% of the tested amplitude, corresponding to a stable 3.1–1132
3.4% attenuation. This is disclosed as a conditional response calibration, not rounded to exact1133
unity. Agreement across strengths is not counted multiple times because the same 100 background1134
realizations are reused.1135
