D Historical Kernel and Piecewise Studies2270
The production workﬂow in the main text uses a Constant ×RBF covariance model with resolution-2271
scaled, dataset-dependent length-scale bounds. This appendix records earlier global, piecewise, and2272
alternative-kernel studies. They are method-development cross-checks, not competing result lanes2273
and not inputs to the v3 upper limit.2274
D.1 Covariance and uncertainty propagation2275
Predictive and statistical uncertainties may be added only when they are expressed as covariances2276
in the same variable. If Cmodel is a conditioned background covariance and Cstat is an independent2277
observation-noise covariance in that same representation, then2278
Ctot = Cmodel + Cstat, σ tot,i =
√
(Ctot)ii. (95)
Standard deviations are not added under the square root. For a GP with training covariance K,2279
cross-covariance K∗, test covariance K∗∗, and heteroscedastic training-noise covariance Σ n, the2280
latent predictive covariance is2281
Cf = K∗∗− KT
∗ (K + Σ n)− 1K∗. (96)
The production implementation assigns sideband statistical noise in the GP training covariance and2282
propagates the conditioned prediction to count space. An additional WhiteKernel variance must2283
therefore not be added again unless it represents a distinct noise contribution in the same space.2284

D.3 Initial global ﬁt of the 2016 10% sample2294
An early global model used an RBF kernel multiplied by a dot-product kernel and added a white-2295
noise term. This construction gave an adequate descriptive ﬁt but was unstable when reused across2296
the local upper-limit scan. It is preserved only as historical motivation for the later stationary2297
Constant×RBF baseline and local blind-window procedure. The lower panel of the historical export2298
shows standardized residuals, which were used to judge the overall descriptive ﬁt; satisfactory global2299
residuals were not suﬃcient to ensure stable interpolation at every scanned mass.2300
D.4 Historical piecewise ﬁtting and kernel weighting2301
The subsequent piecewise study trained a separate GP around each mass hypothesis, withholding2302
a central region from the background ﬁt. Its kernel ensemble converted the log marginal likelihood2303
ℓk of each candidate Kk to2304
wk = exp(ℓk − maxj ℓj)∑
j exp(ℓj − maxj ℓj) . (102)
A kernel was then sampled from these weights before producing a blind-region prediction. This2305
model-averaging experiment predates the frozen single-kernel likelihood and is not used in the2306
current scan. The sampling step made the inferred background depend on both candidate-kernel2307
likelihoods and a stochastic kernel choice; the v3 analysis instead freezes one kernel family and2308
validates its bounded length scale with pseudoexperiments.2309
The associated signal-yield plot used a direct known-background Poisson construction, not the2310
proﬁle-likelihood CL s method used in v3. With ﬁxed background expectation b, observation n, and2311
one-sided tail probability α, that historical construction was2312
µup = 1
2 F − 1
χ 2
2(n+1)
(1 − α), s up = max(0, µ up − b). (103)
The displayed study used α = 0 . 05. It is retained only to identify the provenance of the old plot;2313
no conversion between this 95% construction and the v3 90% CL result is performed.2314
D.5 Guard-band construction reference2315
The guard-band construction considered during the 2015 closure campaign separated each mass2316
hypothesis into a central blind/extraction window, an adjacent band omitted from both training2317
and extraction, and outer GP-training sidebands. The central window covered |m − m0|< 1. 64 σm,2318
while the guard excluded 1. 64 < |m − m0|/σ m < 2. 25. The matched geometry study in Section B.32319
selects the 2. 25 σm blind/extraction window because it preserves the same training protection while2320
retaining the signal tails in the extraction likelihood.2321
D.6 Exploratory cross-kernel closure2322
An exploratory protocol ﬁt the 2016 10% spectrum with RBF and Matern32 kernels, generated 3002323
background-only toys from each ﬁtted model, and extracted each ensemble with the other kernel2324
after Gaussian signal injection. That study was designed to expose misspeciﬁcation through pull2325
bias. No frozen coverage table or production-matched upper-limit export from this comparison is2326
used in v3, so it is not cited as evidence for the baseline kernel choice.2327

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
Figure 156: Historical global ﬁt to the 2016 10% sample using an RBF kernel multiplied by a linear
DotProduct kernel, with an added WhiteKernel term. The lower panel gives the standardized
residuals used in the original study to assess the ﬁt. Although the global description was acceptable,
repeated use in the mass scan was unstable; the ﬁgure therefore motivates the later local excluded-
window construction and is not the v3 Constant ×RBF background model.

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
E Comparison of the Corrected 2015 Polynomial Re-analysis and2328
the GPR Method2329
This appendix compares a historical 95% CL GPR-based 2015 upper limit with the corrected 20152330
re-analysis documented in Appendix J of the 2016 bump-hunt internal note [ 28]. The comparison is2331
useful because both analyses use the same fully unblinded 2015 engineering-run mass spectrum, the2332
same basic prompt A′→ e+e− signal hypothesis, and closely related detector inputs for the mass2333
resolution and radiative normalization. At the same time, the comparison should not be interpreted2334
as a proof that the two statistical procedures are equivalent. The corrected 2015 re-analysis is a2335
local windowed polynomial ﬁt, while the present analysis uses a sideband-trained Gaussian process2336
to predict the blinded mass window and propagates the resulting correlated background uncertainty2337
through the proﬁled likelihood.2338
E.1 Historical 2015 and 2016 polynomial analyses2339
The published 2015 prompt resonance search used the full 2015 engineering-run data set passing the2340
analysis selection and scanned the invariant-mass spectrum from roughly 19 MeV to 81 MeV [3, 31].2341
For each mass hypothesis, the local model consisted of a Gaussian signal component plus a smooth2342
background described by a Chebyshev polynomial of the ﬁrst kind. The published optimization2343
selected a ﬁfth-order polynomial below about 39 MeV and a third-order polynomial above that mass,2344
with broad mass windows tied to the detector resolution. Upper limits were obtained with a proﬁle-2345
likelihood construction and a power-constrained prescription: when the unconstrained observed2346
limit became stronger than the median background-only sensitivity, the quoted signal-yield limit2347
was set to the median expected limit [ 31]. The corresponding coupling limit used the standard2348
ﬁxed-target relation between the signal yield and the radiative trident rate, with the 2015 radiative2349
fraction convention frad = 0. 085.2350
The 2016 bump-hunt analysis generalized the polynomial approach. It used a Gaussian signal2351
component plus a Legendre-polynomial background in a mass window scaled to [−1, 1], so that the2352
polynomial basis was orthogonal over the ﬁtted interval. The polynomial order and window size2353
were selected separately by mass, using 10% data and toy studies to check the background-only ﬁt2354
quality, the ﬁtted signal pull, and the expected limit behavior. The 2016 note then used a bounded2355
proﬁle-likelihood CL s construction for the upper limit, with systematic variations propagated by2356
repeated ﬁts or conservative conversion factors [ 28]. The “Legendre Polynomial Approach” curve2357
used in Fig. 160 is the corrected 2015 re-analysis from that 2016 code path and Appendix J, not2358
the original published 2015 contour.2359
E.2 Implementation issues in the original 2015 result2360
Appendix J of the 2016 internal note identiﬁes two separate implementation errors in the original2361
2015 bump-hunt code [ 28]. First, the local ﬁt window was scaled to [−0. 25, 0. 25] rather than to2362
the intended [−1, 1]. This was not a harmless convention change: the polynomial background basis2363
was chosen on the assumption that it would be orthogonal over the ﬁtted coordinate range, so2364
the incorrect scaling broke the orthogonality argument and invalidated the original model-selection2365
basis. Second, the mass resolution was parameterized in the unscaled mass coordinate but was not2366
consistently transformed into the scaled ﬁt coordinate. The signal template width entering the ﬁt2367
was therefore inconsistent with the coordinate system used by the likelihood.2368
After these corrections, the model selection preferred narrower and more stable windows for the2369
190

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
2015 re-analysis:2370

{
O(5), n σ = 10, m A′ < 40 MeV,

The corrected re-analysis found no signiﬁcant excess and produced the revised ε2 limit shown in2371
Fig. 71 of the 2016 internal note. Figure 72 of that note compares the original and corrected 20152372
contours directly.2373
This issue should be distinguished from the RooFit binned extended-maximum-likelihood prob-2374
lem discussed in the 2015 internal note [ 31]. That RooFit problem produced biases in high-2375
occupancy binned ﬁts with several ﬂoating parameters, but the 2015 internal note states that the2376
ﬁnal results used the independent vanilla ROOT ﬁtter. The Appendix J corrections are therefore2377
a separate coordinate-scaling and signal-width consistency problem in the 2015 resonance-search2378
implementation.2379
E.3 Present GPR method2380
The GPR method is not a polynomial re-ﬁt with a smoother background curve. For each test mass,2381
the method excludes the blinded region from the training data, ﬁts the smooth invariant-mass2382
spectrum in log-count space, and predicts the count-space mean vector and covariance matrix in the2383
blinded mass window. The local signal extraction then uses a multi-bin Poisson likelihood in which2384
the GP-predicted background covariance is represented by correlated Gaussian-constrained nuisance2385
parameters. A full-range Gaussian signal template is normalized over the histogram support and2386
then sliced into the blinded window without re-normalization, so the ﬁtted signal amplitude remains2387
the total local Gaussian yield associated with the mass hypothesis. The comparison curve uses a2388
95% CL upper limit obtained with the bounded proﬁle-likelihood CL s construction described in2389
Section 4; it is retained as a historical cross-check and is not the v3 90% CL candidate.2390
For the observed-limit comparison shown here, the GPR curve corresponds to an earlier 20152391
conﬁguration with a widened 2. 25 σm blind and training exclusion region, a 1 MeV scan step, the2392
7% radiative-normalization penalty, and the ε2 density evaluated with the 1. 64 σm convention. The2393
validation studies in Section 5 motivate the widened blind/extraction window through leakage, pull,2394
amplitude-recovery, and signiﬁcance-calibration diagnostics. This ﬁgure is not used to deﬁne the2395
v3 conﬁdence-level convention or the simultaneous candidate.2396
E.4 Observed-limit comparison2397

with the observed 2015 GPR limit. The historical curve was digitized from the solid black contour2399
in Fig. 71 of the 2016 internal note, with the digitized points and overlay QA archived together2400
with the comparison plot. Over the common 20 MeV to 81 MeV interval, the ratio2401

E.5 Interpretation2407
The close observed-limit agreement is reassuring, but its meaning is limited. An observed ε2 limit is2408
driven by the local event density, the radiative-fraction normalization, the mass-resolution model,2409
and the realized upward or downward ﬂuctuations in the same data. Those inputs are largely2410
shared between the two 2015 curves, so agreement in the gross sensitivity scale is expected if neither2411
background model introduces a large bias. The agreement therefore supports the statement that2412
the GPR procedure is operating on the correct physical normalization scale and is not obviously2413
over- or under-excluding the 2015 data relative to the corrected historical analysis.2414
The agreement is not, by itself, a validation of coverage, background-model bias, or absence2415
of signal absorption. Those questions are addressed by the toy program: functional-form pseudo-2416
experiments test closure against smooth analytic spectra, GP-propagated pseudoexperiments test2417
behavior under the GP background model, and reﬁt-on-toy studies test whether the sideband train-2418
ing can absorb signal-like structure. The comparison in Fig. 160 should therefore be presented2419
as a cross-check of the observed sensitivity scale. The functional-form closure studies and the2420
outstanding production-matched coverage test provide the relevant calibration evidence and gate,2421
respectively.2422
The corrected 2015 re-analysis also changes how the published 2015 result should be used in2423
this note. The original published contour should not be used as the primary target for validating2424
the GPR background model, because Appendix J shows that the original coordinate scaling and2425
signal-width treatment were inconsistent with the ﬁt model assumptions. The corrected re-analysis2426
is the more appropriate historical baseline. At the same time, the corrected analysis did not2427
reveal a signiﬁcant 2015 excess, so the physics conclusion that the 2015 engineering run did not2428
contain a discovery-level prompt resonance remains unchanged. The practical implication is that2429
comparisons to 2015 should emphasize the corrected polynomial result for methodology checks,2430
while the published 2015 exclusion remains part of the historical HPS record.2431

reduced signal leakage into the sideband ﬁt. The v3 matched-geometry choice instead uses a 2. 25 σm
blind/extraction window, which protects the training sample while retaining the Gaussian signal
tails in the likelihood.

F Version 4.2 Follow-up Studies: Compact Record2432
Finding summary . Fixed-mass common-binning and physical-proﬁle checks did not moti-
vate a change to the accepted v4.2 card. Conditional replacements of the 2021 65 MeV central
window were useful mechanism diagnostics, but their answer depended on the frozen replace-
ment construction and they supplied neither expected bands nor a global-null calibration.
Version 4.9 therefore does not use the replacement-window ensemble as headline validation
evidence.
2433
The complete v4.8.3-era appendix source is preserved verbatim at archive/appendix_v4p2_2434
followups_v4p8p3_full.tex. That archival source contains all original tables, ﬁgures, seeds, and2435
post-ﬁgure follow-ups. It is intentionally not compiled into the v4.9 decision path.2436
F.1 Common-binning and proﬁled 65 MeV extraction2437
The ﬁxed-mass follow-up documented that exact 0.625 MeV common bins could not be formed2438
from the histogram-only 2015 and 2016 inputs. A source-compatible 0.5 MeV reﬁt and a broader2439
1.25 MeV stress test preserved the qualitative 65 MeV extraction. The physical-domain proﬁle2440
display also separated the nonnegative interval used for limits from the signed Wald coordinate2441
used for local extraction. These were observed-data robustness checks, not a choice made from toy2442
coverage.2443
F.2 Conditional central-window replacement provenance2444
Two ﬁxed-seed pseudo-observations replaced the 2021 central region and reran the complete moving-2445
mask analysis. A later deterministic functional-mean lane separated a coherent source-truth/GP2446
shoulder below 65 MeV from the additional Poisson ﬂuctuation of one draw. Ten ﬁxed-GP-mean2447
replacement draws compared the discretely equivalent 2. 25/2. 5σm scope with a 3σm scope. Those2448
ﬁnite draws were correlated, conditional interventions. They did not reﬁt a source generator,2449
establish direct CL s coverage, or measure the probability that the observed 65 MeV feature is2450
background.2451
For v4.9, the relevant lesson is limited: replacing a central window can reveal analysis-mediated2452
response, but it is not a substitute for source-qualiﬁed full-reﬁt functional-form toys. The threshold-2453
support decision is therefore based on the matched-reference study in Section 5, with the older2454
window material retained only for provenance.2455
196

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
G Signal-candidate catalogue and conditional inpainting tests2456
This appendix catalogues upward structures visible in the seven distinct v4.2 upper-limit likelihood2457
curves and tests whether replacing one such structure by a smooth conditional background changes2458
another mass point or a combination containing the altered dataset. The exercise is deliberately2459
a data-selected counterfactual diagnostic . It does not redeﬁne the accepted observed limit, provide2460
a trials-corrected discovery probability, or turn the conditional ﬁxed-GP ensembles into coverage-2461
calibrated bands.2462
G.1 Operational catalogue from the seven limit curves2463
The seven inputs are the three standalone curves, the three pair curves over their genuine overlap2464
intervals, and the all-three curve over 50–90 MeV. The lower-order active-set tails drawn in the union2465
panel are not counted a second time. The catalogue is constructed by the following reproducible2466
screen:2467
1. retain a ﬁnite mass point when its observed 90% CL s upper limit lies strictly above the pointwise2468
97.5% quantile of that curve’s conditional ﬁxed-state ensemble and its signed local excess is2469
positive;2470
2. merge adjacent one-MeV points into a curve-level island and represent the island by its minimum2471
local p0 point;2472
3. merge islands across curves only when every pair of their one-resolution intervals overlaps2473
(complete-link grouping); and2474
4. choose the reference mass from the minimum- p0 point in the highest-order supporting likelihood,2475
preferring all-three, then a genuine pair, then standalone.2476
The ±2. 5σm inpainting windows are not used to merge features; doing so would combine the2477
physically distinct 80 and 90 MeV structures. The 97.5% quantile itself is only an operational2478
graphical threshold: the standalone and pairwise quantiles use 100 conditional draws and the all-2479
three quantile uses 300. Their tail counts, local p0 values, and supporting combinations reuse2480
data and conditional ensembles, so the largest value in a feature is retained only as a descriptive2481
catalogue entry.2482

The resulting eight entries form a ﬁnite catalogue of conditional-limit excursion features and2483
deﬁne the ﬁxed comparison set below. The tight observed limits near 72–75 MeV record the2484
neighboring deﬁcit/background structure; their proximity to the 65 and 80 MeV regions makes the2485
moving-mask response especially nonlocal in this interval.2486
G.2 Native-histogram candidate-window inpainting protocol2487
Every catalogue mass is tested in every dataset active at that mass, giving 19 candidate–dataset2488
interventions. For each intervention, complete post-rebin analysis bins are selected by centers inside2489
the dataset-speciﬁc continuous mc ± 2. 5σm(mc) interval. Their corresponding native 0.05, 0.05, or2490
0.125 MeV bins are replaced; every native bin outside the selected interval remains bitwise identical2491
to the source histogram. Thus both the rebinned GP counts and the uncropped count-density2492
integral used for the ε2 conversion see the altered integer histogram.2493
The generating kernel constant and length scale are ﬁxed to the reviewed v4.2 state at mc, but2494
the GP posterior is reconditioned after excluding the full ±2. 5σm interval so that the removed fea-2495
ture cannot enter the generating training sample. The stochastic construction draws the complete2496
vector of excised analysis-bin intensities jointly from the count-space GP posterior, using the same2497
nonnegative reject_then_clip convention as the conditional-band generator, and then draws Pois-2498
son analysis-bin totals. Smooth within-analysis-bin weights allocate each total multinomially to2499
native bins. Three such draws are retained with matched replicate labels. A fourth, separate lane2500
rounds the posterior mean explicitly and uses largest-remainder native-bin allocation without a2501
Poisson draw. That deterministic lane is a central reference and is not one of the three replicates.2502
Each modiﬁed histogram is reused unchanged in the altered standalone likelihood, every pair2503
containing the altered dataset, and the all-active combination. Likelihoods excluding that dataset2504
are exact no-change controls by construction. The inference step retains the accepted v4.2 ±2. 25σm2505
moving mask, physical density window, 12 optimizer restarts, asymptotic CL s calibration, and2506
shared-ε2 combination. Nearby catalogue anchors within 30 MeV are tested for every intervention;2507
the 2021 removals at 65 and 80 MeV additionally use a one-MeV 55–95 MeV grid.2508
This pilot contains 76 native histograms, 564 optimized single-dataset ﬁts, and 2140 aﬀected-2509
scope likelihood results. Every ﬁt is ﬁnite. One deterministic 2021 ﬁt initially selected a length-scale2510
boundary; three unchanged-card repeats found the same higher-likelihood interior family and the2511
maximum ﬁnite-LML solution was selected. A subsequent branch audit identiﬁed nine additional2512
non-bound but substantially lower-LML states. Three independent unchanged-card repeats per2513
state found reproducible higher- LML interior solutions for all nine; the maximum ﬁnite-LML2514
solution was again selected, with the other 554 single-ﬁt rows unchanged. Full attempt ledgers are2515
retained. No ﬁnal state is interpolated or on a kernel bound. Fixed- state reconstructions close in2516
LML to 6. 1 × 10− 6 or better, and all outside-window native counts close bitwise.2517
For compact summaries an eﬀect is labelled “appreciable” when2518

Values are mechanism diagnostics, not calibrated probabilities.
The matrix shows that the ﬁtted response extends beyond the replaced signal window. The2522
strongest, clearest example is the reciprocal 65/80 MeV test in 2021. At 80 MeV the ﬁtted log-2523

correlation is approximately exp[− 1
2 (0. 208/ 0. 304)2] = 0 . 79. Thus the two windows are separated on2525
the signal-resolution scale while both remain within the nonlocal GP correlation range and each can2526
enter the other’s moving-mask training sample. The broad adjacent deﬁcit/background structure2527
can therefore transmit a reciprocal change when the common proﬁle is rerun. This is a statement2528
about the reﬁtting procedure rather than a physical dependence between the two local features.2529

production. Its full event-level directory is not locally mounted. The available full-statistics kine-2544
matic source is a diﬀerent frozen v11 SIMP/TM atlas with 1186 input ﬁles and 293,397,563 selected2545
rows scanned. The following plots are therefore conditional selected-row mechanism diagnostics;2546
they are not the kinematics of the exact events entering the observed v4.2 limit, a ﬁtted candidate2547
yield, or an independent signiﬁcance.2548
The central interval is the physical 2021 65 ± 2. 5σm = [59 . 696, 70. 304] MeV window. The2549
nominal two sidebands span 2.5–4.0 σm and are scaled by mass width. Stopping at 4 σm keeps the2550
right sideband below the 80 MeV candidate’s own ±2. 5σm interval. Four stored kinematic surfaces2551
are available: pair momentum sum, reconstructed vertex z, vertex-projection signiﬁcance, and the2552
minimum-track |y0|proxy.2553

The available v11 atlas therefore supplies a concrete instrumental/selection hypothesis—2554
especially the L2L2 concentration—and motivates the sample-matched exact-v16 comparisons2555
added below. A candidate-level kinematic inference additionally requires unique-event semantics,2556
category-speciﬁc signal response, and a correlation-preserving background treatment.2557
G.5 Descriptive synthesis and follow-up2558
The 65 MeV region remains the most informative interior follow-up because it has the broadest2559
positional recurrence and largest all-three local excess. The 51 MeV feature has exact-position2560
recurrence in 2015 and 2021 while touching the 2021 lower search rail; the 90 MeV feature recurs in2561
2015 and 2016 at their upper overlap rail; and 117 MeV is a broad 2016-only structure. The 80 MeV2562
region is retained as its own local feature and as a neighboring background-proﬁle stress region.2563
Reciprocal inpainting/reﬁt response near 65 and 80 MeV is model-mediated through the shared2564
nonlocal GP/proﬁle and adjacent deﬁcit structure; it is not a claim that one feature physically2565
depends on the other.2566
The corresponding follow-up program is a predeclared joint 65+80 union excision, denser2567
unchanged-card optimizer-repeat review of the aﬀected interval, full-procedure signal-injection and2568
background-only coverage studies with the same moving-mask reﬁt, scan-wise calibration, and an2569
event-identiﬁed v16 kinematic comparison. The present appendix supplies the catalogue and mech-2570
anism screen used to deﬁne those tests.2571
G.6 Exact-v16 selected-row morphology diagnostics2572
The preceding kinematic atlas used a v11 SIMP/TM production because the exact event-level2573
source of the v4.2 observed-limit histogram was not then locally available. A new v16 diagnostic2574
production now supplies the unchanged prompt-target-constrained, psum > 2. 8 baseline together2575
with exhaustive topology and stored-trigger partitions and four mass-versus-control histograms.2576
This closes the earlier histogram-level sample mismatch: the 2000-, 4000-, and 8000-bin invariant-2577
mass histograms, including their axes, are bitwise identical to the frozen v4.2 2021 input and each2578
contains 141,590,915 selected rows. Unique physical-event counting remains outside this histogram-2579
level closure because the parser did not identify a stable run/event key.2580

G.6.1 Sideband-trained conditional-composition GPR2587
The available histograms do not support an event-level two-dimensional signal likelihood. In par-2588
ticular, there is no signal template in the second coordinate, no category-speciﬁc eﬃciency or mass-2589
resolution model, and no validated factorization of selected rows into independent events. The2590
useful part of the true-muonium 2D-GPR construction is therefore adapted only as a background-2591
morphology diagnostic: a target-masked smooth surface is ﬁt to the mass dependence of the condi-2592
tional control composition.2593
For mass bin i and one of J mutually represented control groups, let Nij be the selected-row2594
count and Ni = ∑
j Nij. The ﬁtted coordinates are the Jeﬀreys-regularized centered log ratios2595
pij = Nij + 1/ 2
Ni + J/ 2 , y ij = log pij − 1
J
J∑
k=1
log pik. (106)
Independent mass-axis RBF predictors for the yij coordinates are coupled again by the inverse2596
centered-log-ratio transform, so every predicted composition sums to one. This conditions on the2597
observed mass-bin total and tests morphology only; it neither estimates the total background under2598
a candidate nor assigns a signal yield.2599
The primary card uses 0.5-MeV mass bins over 40–160 MeV and eight contiguous control groups2600
whose boundaries are frozen from candidate-masked sideband rows. The eight stored 3D-hit bins,2601
six exhaustive topology categories, and three nonempty trigger categories remain discrete. The2602
union of the ﬁxed 65± 2. 5σm and 80± 2. 5σm stripes is excluded from both kernel choice and training2603
to protect the interpolation against reciprocal model-mediated cross-response. Such cross-response2604
207

HPS-ANALYSIS-NOTE BH-GPR v4.9.7
can arise through the common nonlocal GP/background proﬁle and adjacent deﬁcit/background2605
structure. It is assigned to the shared ﬁtting procedure rather than to physical causation, shared2606
events, or intrinsic dependence between the features. For each surface the RBF length and stan-2607
dardized nugget are chosen from2608
ℓm ∈ {2, 4, 8, 12, 20, 35} MeV, η ∈ {10− 4, 10− 3, 10− 2, 5 × 10− 2}, (107)
using the median centered-log-ratio residual in ﬁve same-rule held-out fake gaps at 50, 100, 115,2609
130, and 145 MeV. None of those scores is converted to a p-value or signiﬁcance. Deterministic2610
checks repeat the procedure at 0.125, 0.25, 0.5, and 1.0 MeV mass binning, with 6, 8, 10, and 122611
continuous control groups, and with single-target rather than union masks. No pseudoexperiment,2612
expected band, or coverage claim is introduced in v4.3.2613

The parent v4.2 deterministic inpainting supplies a useful scale for the dilution of any local2623
component. In its complete analysis-bin windows, (Ndata − NGP mean )/N data is 0.2605% at 65 MeV2624
and 0.1460% at 80 MeV. These are conditional mean-ﬁll diﬀerences, not signal purities. The parent2625
counterfactual changes each complete window total by less than 0.3%, which illustrates the severe2626
dilution scale but does not identify the remaining rows as background. Sub-percent composition2627
changes therefore cannot by themselves distinguish a dilute prompt signal from ordinary mass2628
dependence or reconstruction eﬀects.2629

The 65 MeV stripe has modest, repeatable changes in some momentum-sum, topology, and high-2630
|y0|fractions, while the trigger composition is nearly unchanged. The detailed vertex-signiﬁcance2631
and 3D-hit-bin directions are less stable under reasonable binning and grouping choices. At 80 MeV2632
the eﬀects are generally smaller and their leading bins or signs change more often across the2633
deterministic variations. Because each comparison reuses the same selected rows and conditions2634
on the mass-bin total, diﬀerences across variables or regions are correlated descriptors rather than2635
separate tests. In particular, the joint mask does not convert shared-model cross-response into a2636
statement about the physical relation between the 65 and 80 MeV features.2637
Thus the exact-v16 product removes the earlier histogram-level sample mismatch and provides2638
a controlled view of saved-variable morphology. The histograms alone do not furnish an event-level2639
signal template, ﬁtted yield, or calibrated local or global signiﬁcance. Event-identiﬁed run/time2640
splits, category-speciﬁc signal simulation and response, and correlation-preserving inference would2641
be required for those quantities.2642

G.7 Exact-v16 candidate-region variable comparisons2643
The exact-v16 mass–control surfaces permit one common regional comparison at six catalogue2644
anchors inside their support: 51, 65, 80, 90, 117, and 162 MeV. The 21 and 39 MeV entries are2645
omitted solely because these surfaces begin at 40 MeV; the omission is a surface-support choice and2646
not an ordering of the catalogue features. The displayed quantities are overlap-weighted histogram2647
approximations to normalized selected-row shapes and category fractions. They do not deﬁne a2648
ﬁtted signal yield, signiﬁcance, or limit.2649
For anchor m0 and the frozen resolution σm(m0), deﬁne the half-open lower, central, and upper2650
regions2651
L(m0) = [ m0 − 4σm, m 0 − 2. 5σm),
C(m0) = [ m0 − 2. 5σm, m 0 + 2. 5σm),
U(m0) = [ m0 + 2. 5σm, m 0 + 4σm).
(108)
For native 0.125-MeV mass bin i with edges [ai, b i), its contribution to region R is weighted by2652
wiR = |[ai, b i) ∩ R|
bi − ai
. (109)
Thus a region total or control-bin count is a fractional edge-overlap sum, NRj = ∑
i wiRNij,2653
rather than a native-bin-center selection. Every regional distribution is normalized independently,2654
pRj = NRj/ ∑
k NRk, so the comparison concerns shape rather than the rapidly varying mass yield.2655
Because the two sidebands have equal width, their symmetric ﬁrst-order local reference is the2656
composition chord2657
pchord,j = pL,j + pU ,j
2 , ∆ j = 100 (pC,j − pchord,j ) , TV = 1
2
∑
j
|pC,j − pchord,j |. (110)
Here ∆ j is a percentage-point shape residual and TV is a descriptive distance. Neither is a pull or2658
calibrated test statistic.2659
For continuous controls, display groups are frozen from rows outside the union of all six fractional2660
central regions and then reused without retuning at individual anchors. This prevents any one2661
catalogue window from setting the display-bin edges. The six surfaces reuse selected rows, so2662
comparisons across variables are correlated descriptions of the same production.2663
The 65 and 80 MeV central windows are disjoint: the ﬁrst ends at 70.304 MeV and the second2664
begins at 74.280 MeV. Their adjacent sidebands overlap from 70.849 to 73.487 MeV and therefore2665
sample part of the same neighboring deﬁcit/background interval. The 80 and 90 MeV central2666
windows overlap from 83.950 to 85.720 MeV, and their adjacent sidebands also cross into the2667
neighboring central interval. The anchor panels are consequently regional views, not a partition2668
into disjoint selected-row samples.2669
For validation at 65 and 80 MeV, the baseline-closed momentum-sum, electron-hit, topology, and2670
trigger surfaces have overlap-weighted central totals of 16,361,498 and 20,983,869 rows, compared2671
with the parser-exact totals of 16,361,818 and 20,984,092. The corresponding overlap-weighted2672
combined-sideband totals are 9,193,205 and 12,256,152 rows, compared with parser-exact values2673
of 9,193,162 and 12,255,727. These few- 10− 5 relative diﬀerences are the expected consequence of2674
applying continuous window edges to saved mass histograms. The vertex-signiﬁcance and minimum-2675
|y0|surfaces retain their separately recorded control-axis ﬂow qualiﬁcations; their per-surface totals2676
are given in the machine-readable ledger.2677

The summary in Figure 179 makes the mass ordering quantitative. At 51 MeV, the chord2687
TV distances are 3.99% for momentum sum, 2.13% for vertex-projection signiﬁcance, 1.04% for2688
minimum |y0|, and 6.05% for electron hit count; this is the strongest left-to-right evolution and2689
coincides with the lower-boundary/selection turn-on region. At 65 MeV the largest distances are2690
2.45% in momentum sum and 1.81% in hit count, with 0.46% in topology and 0.31% in stored-2691
trigger composition. The corresponding 80 MeV distances are smaller: 0.63%, 0.92%, 0.41%, and2692
0.10%, respectively. The 90 and 117 MeV central shapes closely interpolate their adjacent sidebands.2693
At 162 MeV the control and topology distances remain small, with 0.49% in hit count and 0.33% in2694
the stored-trigger composition; this anchor is retained as a direct morphology comparison beyond2695
the current 40–160 MeV primary conditional-GPR domain.2696
The reciprocal 65/80 response reported by the frozen v4.2 inpainting/reﬁt is interpreted sep-2697
arately from these direct shape comparisons. It is a model-mediated response of the common2698
nonlocal GP/background proﬁle and can be transmitted by the adjacent deﬁcit/background struc-2699
ture. The present plots therefore retain 65 and 80 MeV as distinct local regions while avoiding a2700
physical-dependence interpretation.2701
The region comparisons keep both adjacent sidebands visible and use one frozen grouping2702
rule across anchors. This supports like-for-like visual and numerical comparisons while retaining2703
ordinary mass evolution in the record. Interpretation remains limited to the saved histogram2704
axes and their selected-row population; the plots do not supply an event-level signal response or2705
inferential calibration.2706

H Archived ten-toy source/exposure studies2707
This appendix preserves the complete v4.1 source/exposure length-scale pilot and the v4.5 ten-2708
toy refmatched continuation in their circulated form. They are retained for provenance, including2709
the raw optimizer pathologies that motivated the predeclared repeat gate in Section B.5. Their2710
numerical rows and plots are not pooled with the uniformly rerun full-100 results.2711
H.1 Ten-toy 2021 source-and-exposure length-scale pilot2712
The pilot implements Eqs. ( 78) and ( 79) for the native 1%, 1% × 10, 1% × 100, native 10%, and2713
10% × 10 scenarios. Ten background pseudoexperiments are generated for each analytic truth and2714
scenario. Within a source family, the same toy index identiﬁes a nested Poisson realization, and the2715
optimizer seed is held common across upper-bound factors at ﬁxed truth, scenario, toy, and mass.2716
This pairing reduces comparison noise; it does not make the independently derived native-1% and2717
native-10% source functions identical.2718
The common-family primary lane uses fGenGammaThresh for both native sources so that the2719
1% × 10 versus native-10% comparison probes the source spectrum and selection rather than a2720
change of analytic family. The fSigPowExpQ lane is kept separate as an alternate-truth stress test.2721
Both are smooth stress truths with their source-ﬁt diagnostics retained in the provenance ledger;2722
neither is promoted as a physical background generator.2723
Recovered 2016 10% observed-input provenance. The original remote 2016 10% ROOT2724
input is not present in the local checkout. The observed histogram used here was recovered from2725
ﬁve retained functional-form products. All ﬁve archived copies have bitwise-identical bin values2726
and edges, with the joint values-and-edges SHA-256 db85ff94c74855549c45b173116b36a70a41832727
ac86db8f374d5b3202c1410656. The source paths, per-ﬁle hashes, underﬂow/overﬂow accounting,2728
recovered-ﬁle hash, and the statement of this limitation are recorded in study_results/v4p1_22729
021_ls_exposure_ensembles_20260804/derived/2016_10pct_recovery_manifest.json . The2730
recovery changes no bin content and is used only to place the 10% and full samples on the same2731
factor-12 wide-support card.2732
On the matched observed grids, increasing the data fraction did not move the optimized length2733
scale upward. For 2016, the median ℓopt/σ x changes from 10.782 in the recovered 10% sample to2734
9.716 in the full sample on the common factor-12 card; the median pointwise change is −1. 080.2735
For 2021, it changes from 13.061 at 1% to 12.060 at 10% on the common factor-15 card, with a2736
median pointwise change of −0. 850 and a negative change at every matched mass. These observed2737
comparisons show that higher statistics do not by themselves require a longer correlation scale.2738
They are diagnostics of these spectra, not an exposure-scaling law.2739

Reviewed background-only pilot result. All 600 predeclared scan tasks and 6600 mass rows2740

Fixed-amplitude sensitivity and bias. For the projected native-1% ×100 and native-10%×102755
lanes, respectively, the median ﬁtted-uncertainty ratios σA(kmax = 20)/σ A(kmax = 15) are 0.9999932756
and 1.00001 in the primary truth lane, and 1.00000 and 0.985677 in the alternate lane. Thus no local2757
extraction-sensitivity loss is seen when lifting the ceiling from factor 15 to factor 20. The alternate2758
native-10%×10 lane instead has a 1.43% smaller ﬁtted uncertainty. Together with the nonbinding2759
factor-20/factor-25 likelihood plateau, this supports factor 20 as the provisional projected-100%2760
ceiling.2761
At factor 20, the paired response medians ( ˆAz − ˆA0)/A inj span 0.971921–0.979603 across the four2762
projected truth/scenario groups and are essentially unchanged at factors 15 and 25. The common2763
2–3% under-response is therefore not introduced by lifting the length-scale ceiling. At factor 20,2764
the absolute anchor-normalized median residual is at most 0.214502 and the median pull widths2765
span 1.01892–1.21408. With only ten independent background toys per scenario, these oﬀsets and2766
widths are an unresolved closure diagnostic rather than evidence for a calibrated bias.2767
The response study uses the ﬁxed absolute amplitudes of Eq. ( 80); it constructs no expected-2768
limit band and does not establish coverage. The raw qµ ﬁelds are neither relabeled nor used by2769
the postprocessor and are explicitly non-promotable, as are the immutable-path ε2 values. None2770
of these pilot quantities supplies an exclusion, global discovery probability, or production-card2771
qualiﬁcation.2772
H.1.1 Frozen-v4.2 refmatched injection–extraction continuation2773
The preceding scan asked how a preﬁt ﬁxed-amplitude response changed as the length-scale ceiling2774
was moved. The v4.5 continuation asks a diﬀerent, production-matched question while keeping the2775
requested candidate ceiling ﬁxed at kmax = 15. It reuses only the reviewed Section 5.4 background2776
histograms for toy indices 0–9. It does not reuse the earlier injection rows, whose masses were2777
60, 120, and 220 MeV and whose absolute amplitudes were anchored to a factor-15 preﬁt Asimov2778
reference. Every result in this continuation is a new full-reﬁt extraction under the declared v4.22779
2021 state.2780
The card uses the 40–300 MeV GP support and 50–250 MeV search range, kmin = 1. 1, kmax = 15,2781
the 2. 25σm blind and training exclusion, the two-sideband requirement, ﬁve-bin local rebinning, and2782
12 optimizer restarts. The four headline ensembles are 1%× 10, native 10%, 1%× 100, and 10%× 10.2783
For all four we use the common fGenGammaThresh analytic family, ﬁtted independently to the native2784
1% and native 10% source spectra. This keeps the functional family common across the requested2785
comparisons; the ﬁts are smooth stress truths and not physical background generators.2786

[0. 5, 2], with range 0.0344–40.61. Eleven of them retain the optimizer-start signature while still2810
reporting a ﬁnite success ﬂag. The latter ﬂag therefore establishes only that the code returned a2811
ﬁnite result without fallback; it is not a likelihood-optimum or repeat-stability test.2812
The independent physics/statistics review classiﬁes this as a completed screening-level con-2813
ditional closure study , not a production qualiﬁcation. Artifact and provenance QA passes, but2814
statistical-closure and optimizer-stability acceptance are held. The retained 65 MeV spurious-2815
signal outlier, the 180 MeV matched-reference branch, the native-10% truth misdescription, and2816
the clustered factor-15 contacts motivate repeat-stability and larger-ensemble checks. No post-hoc2817
row removal, interpolation, or enlargement of the candidate length-scale bound is applied.2818
Finally, a separate atomic ROOT product extends the deterministic Section 5.4 nested-Poisson2819
streams through indices 10–99 for both predeclared truth lanes and all ﬁve source/exposure scenar-2820
ios. All 100 original index-0–9 histograms reproduce bit-for-bit. The 90 new toys per truth/scenario2821
are generated but are not used in any v4.5 number or ﬁgure, and their extraction is not authorized2822
by the present statistical review. Before continuation, a deterministic matched-reference and repeat-2823
stability gate must be predeclared, including archived seeds and a ﬁxed likelihood-selection criterion.2824
Pooling them later requires the present card, summary schema, and outlier policy to remain frozen;2825
a scientiﬁc protocol change instead requires a new versioned study.2826

Figure 186: Standalone zero-signal, or spurious-signal, result. Each row is one source/exposure
ensemble; the columns show the ten raw signed ˆε2 coordinates at each mass, the arithmetic pull

and width 3.187 because toy 1 has pull −9. 867. That pseudoexperiment is retained as the principal
spurious-signal stability warning. Error bars on pull means are sample-standard-error estimates
and those on widths use the ﬁnite-Gaussian approximation; with ten toys they are guides, not
coverage intervals.

Figure 191: Raw and median signed ε2 extraction coordinates for the 1-, 3-, and 5- σA injections.
Each panel contains ten ﬁtted points, the empirical 16–84% interval around their median, and the
corresponding toy-local injected coordinates. Symmetric-log ordinates retain negative estimates
and rare large ﬁt branches without clipping. Because Kstm and σref
A,stm vary among background
toys, the injected points are themselves distributions rather than one universal horizontal value.
These are extraction-coordinate diagnostics, not ε2 limits or expected bands. Each panel selects
its own symmetric-log range, so apparent vertical spreads should not be compared without the
numerical ledger.

Direct unpaired source/exposure comparisons

Figure 192: Direct unpaired comparisons requested between native 10% and 1% × 10 (top), and
between 10% × 10 and 1% × 100 (bottom). Solid curves and bands are ﬁtted medians and empirical
16–84% intervals; dashed curves are injected medians. The 1%- and 10%-source functions are
independently ﬁtted and their ensembles are independently generated. Moreover, the expected
native-10%/1% × 10 normalization ratio is 1.1296466, with the same ratio for the scaled pair.
The diﬀerences therefore combine source/selection shape and normalization and are neither pure
exposure eﬀects nor paired tests. The shaded regions are pointwise descriptive quantiles of ten
values, not expected bands, and can suppress isolated extremes; Fig. 191 retains all raw signed
values.
